### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given at $298$ K: $E^{\ominus}_{Fe^{2+}/Fe} = X$ Volt; $E^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe} = Y$ Volt. The $E^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe^{2+}}$ in Volt at $298$ K is given by:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2X-3Y$
- **(B)** $3Y-2X$
- **(C)** $3Y+2X$
- **(D)** $Y+X$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3Y-2X$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The half-reactions and their standard Gibbs free energy changes are given by:

$Fe^{2+} + 2e^{-} \rightarrow Fe$

$\Delta G^{\ominus}_{1} = -2FX$

$Fe^{3+} + 3e^{-} \rightarrow Fe$

$\Delta G^{\ominus}_{2} = -3FY$

The required half-reaction is:

$Fe^{3+} + e^{-} \rightarrow Fe^{2+}$

$\Delta G^{\ominus}_{3} = -1FE^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe^{2+}}$

This reaction is obtained by subtracting the first reaction from the second reaction:

$\Delta G^{\ominus}_{3} = \Delta G^{\ominus}_{2} - \Delta G^{\ominus}_{1}$

$-FE^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe^{2+}} = -3FY - (-2FX)$

$-FE^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe^{2+}} = -3FY + 2FX$

$E^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe^{2+}} = 3Y - 2X$

Answer: $3Y-2X$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For a general redox reaction
Anode : $\text{Red}_1 \rightarrow \text{Ox}_1^{n_1^+} + n_1 e^-$
Cathode : $\text{Ox}_2 + n_2 e^- \rightarrow \text{Red}_2^{n_2^-}$
Which of the following statement is incorrect ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** The overall reaction can be written as $n_2\text{Red}_1 + n_1\text{Ox}_2 \rightleftharpoons n_2\text{Ox}_1^{n_1^+} + n_1\text{Red}_2^{n_2^-}$
- **(B)** The electrons do not appear in the overall reaction because electrons produced at the anode are consumed at the cathode.
- **(C)** Here $n$ is the number of electrons transferred in redox reaction. 
<img style="width: 250px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/06APRS2__q57_d1.jpg" />
- **(D)** If the reaction is carried out reversibly, the electrical work done is equal to the ratio of charge and potential difference through which charge is moved.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) If the reaction is carried out reversibly, the electrical work done is equal to the ratio of charge and potential difference through which charge is moved.**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the given redox reaction, the half-cell reactions are:
Anode: $\text{Red}_1 \rightarrow \text{Ox}_1^{n_1^+} + n_1 e^-$
Cathode: $\text{Ox}_2 + n_2 e^- \rightarrow \text{Red}_2^{n_2^-}$

To obtain the overall balanced chemical equation, we multiply the anode reaction by $n_2$ and the cathode reaction by $n_1$ so that the number of electrons lost equals the number of electrons gained:
$n_2\text{Red}_1 \rightarrow n_2\text{Ox}_1^{n_1^+} + n_1 n_2 e^-$
$n_1\text{Ox}_2 + n_1 n_2 e^- \rightarrow n_1\text{Red}_2^{n_2^-}$

Adding these gives the overall reaction:
$n_2\text{Red}_1 + n_1\text{Ox}_2 \rightleftharpoons n_2\text{Ox}_1^{n_1^+} + n_1\text{Red}_2^{n_2^-}$
Thus, statement (A) is correct.

In the overall reaction, electrons do not appear because the electrons produced at the anode are exactly consumed at the cathode. Thus, statement (B) is correct.

From the Nernst equation:
$E = E^\circ - \dfrac{RT}{nF} \ln Q = E^\circ - \dfrac{2.303 RT}{nF} \log_{10} Q$
$\dfrac{E - E^\circ}{RT/F} = -\dfrac{2.303}{n} \log_{10} Q$
The graph of $\dfrac{E - E^\circ}{RT/F}$ versus $\log_{10} Q$ is a straight line with a slope of $-\dfrac{2.303}{n}$, where $n$ is the number of electrons transferred in the balanced redox reaction. Thus, statement (C) is correct.

The electrical work done by a galvanic cell is given by the product of the total charge transferred and the cell potential (potential difference), not their ratio. 
$W_{\text{electrical}} = \text{Charge} \times \text{Potential difference} = nF E_{\text{cell}}$
Thus, statement (D) is incorrect.

Answer: If the reaction is carried out reversibly, the electrical work done is equal to the ratio of charge and potential difference through which charge is moved.

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

One half cell in a voltaic cell is constructed by dipping silver rod in $AgNO_3$ solution of unknown concentration, other half cell is $Zn$ rod dipped in $1$ molar solution of $ZnSO_4$. A voltage of $1.60\text{ V}$ is measured at $298\text{ K}$ for this cell. What is the concentration of $Ag^+$ ions used in terms of $\log x$ ($x = [Ag^+]$) ?
$E^\circ_{Zn^{2+}/Zn} = -0.76\text{ V}$, $E^\circ_{Ag^+/Ag} = +0.80\text{ V}$, $\dfrac{2.303 RT}{F} = 0.059\text{ V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{2}{3.9}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{4}{5.9}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{2.9}{2}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{5.9}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{4}{5.9}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The cell reaction is $Zn(s) + 2Ag^+(aq) \rightarrow Zn^{2+}(aq) + 2Ag(s)$

The standard cell potential is given by:
$E^\circ_{cell} = E^\circ_{cathode} - E^\circ_{anode} = 0.80 - (-0.76) = 1.56\text{ V}$

Using the Nernst equation:
$E_{cell} = E^\circ_{cell} - \dfrac{0.059}{n} \log \dfrac{[Zn^{2+}]}{[Ag^+]^2}$

Substituting the given values ($n = 2$, $[Zn^{2+}] = 1\text{ M}$, $E_{cell} = 1.60\text{ V}$):
$1.60 = 1.56 - \dfrac{0.059}{2} \log \dfrac{1}{x^2}$

$0.04 = - \dfrac{0.059}{2} (-2 \log x)$

$0.04 = 0.059 \log x$

$\log x = \dfrac{0.04}{0.059} = \dfrac{4}{5.9}$

Answer: $\dfrac{4}{5.9}$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An electrochemical cell, consist of the following two redox couples, $M^{x+}(aq)/M(s)$ $[E_{red}^\ominus=+0.15\text{ V}]$ and $Fe^{3+}(aq)/Fe(s)$ $[E_{red}^\ominus=-0.036\text{ V}]$. The cell EMF $(E_{cell})$ is recorded to be $0.2057$ V. If the reaction quotient of the electrochemical reaction is found to be $10^{-2}$, then the value of $x$ is ______. (Nearest integer)
[Given: M is a p-block metal and $\dfrac{2.303RT}{F}=0.059$ V]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given standard reduction potentials:
$E^{\ominus}_{M^{x+}/M} = +0.15$ V
$E^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe} = -0.036$ V

Since $E^{\ominus}_{M^{x+}/M} > E^{\ominus}_{Fe^{3+}/Fe}$, reduction occurs at the $M^{x+}/M$ electrode (cathode) and oxidation occurs at the $Fe^{3+}/Fe$ electrode (anode).

Cathode reaction: $(M^{x+} + x e^{-} \rightarrow M) \times 3$
Anode reaction: $(Fe \rightarrow Fe^{3+} + 3 e^{-}) \times x$

Overall balanced cell reaction:
$3 M^{x+} + x Fe \rightarrow 3 M + x Fe^{3+}$

The number of electrons transferred in the balanced reaction is $n = 3x$.

Standard cell potential $E^{\ominus}_{cell}$ is:
$E^{\ominus}_{cell} = E^{\ominus}_{cathode} - E^{\ominus}_{anode}$
$E^{\ominus}_{cell} = 0.15 - (-0.036) = 0.186$ V

Using the Nernst equation:
$E_{cell} = E^{\ominus}_{cell} - \dfrac{2.303RT}{nF} \log Q$

Substitute the given values ($E_{cell} = 0.2057$ V, $Q = 10^{-2}$, and $\dfrac{2.303RT}{F} = 0.059$ V):
$0.2057 = 0.186 - \dfrac{0.059}{3x} \log(10^{-2})$

$0.2057 - 0.186 = -\dfrac{0.059}{3x} \times (-2)$

$0.0197 = \dfrac{0.118}{3x}$

$3x = \dfrac{0.118}{0.0197}$

$3x \approx 6$

$x = 2$

Answer: $2$

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An electrochemical cell is constructed using half cells in the direction of spontaneous change
$Fe(OH)_2(s) + 2e^- \rightarrow Fe(s) + 2OH^-(aq) \quad E^\theta = -0.88$ V
and $AgBr(s) + e^- \rightarrow Ag(s) + Br^-(aq) \quad E^\theta = +0.07$ V
Which of the following option is <b>correct</b>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Overall reaction $Fe(s) + 2OH^-(aq) + 2AgBr(s) \rightleftharpoons Fe(OH)_2(s) + 2Ag(s) + 2Br^-(aq)$
- **(B)** $E^\theta_{cell} = -0.95$ V
- **(C)** Fe is reduced in the electrochemical cell
- **(D)** $E^\theta_{cell}$ is an extensive property

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Overall reaction $Fe(s) + 2OH^-(aq) + 2AgBr(s) \rightleftharpoons Fe(OH)_2(s) + 2Ag(s) + 2Br^-(aq)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a spontaneous reaction, the cell potential $E^{\theta}_{cell}$ must be positive. The half-cell with the higher standard reduction potential acts as the cathode (reduction), and the one with the lower standard reduction potential acts as the anode (oxidation).

Given standard reduction potentials:
$E^{\theta}_{AgBr/Ag} = +0.07$ V
$E^{\theta}_{Fe(OH)_2/Fe} = -0.88$ V

Since $+0.07$ V $> -0.88$ V, $AgBr$ undergoes reduction at the cathode and $Fe$ undergoes oxidation at the anode.

Cathode reaction: $2AgBr(s) + 2e^{-} \rightarrow 2Ag(s) + 2Br^{-}(aq)$
Anode reaction: $Fe(s) + 2OH^{-}(aq) \rightarrow Fe(OH)_2(s) + 2e^{-}$

Overall cell reaction:
$Fe(s) + 2OH^{-}(aq) + 2AgBr(s) \rightleftharpoons Fe(OH)_2(s) + 2Ag(s) + 2Br^{-}(aq)$

Standard cell potential:
$E^{\theta}_{cell} = E^{\theta}_{cathode} - E^{\theta}_{anode} = 0.07 - (-0.88) = +0.95$ V

$E^{\theta}_{cell}$ is an intensive property, and $Fe$ is oxidized in the cell. Thus, only the overall reaction given in the first option is correct.

Answer: Overall reaction $Fe(s) + 2OH^-(aq) + 2AgBr(s) \rightleftharpoons Fe(OH)_2(s) + 2Ag(s) + 2Br^-(aq)$

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A volume of $x\,\mathrm{mL}$ of $5\,\mathrm{M}\,\mathrm{NaHCO_3}$ solution was mixed with $10\,\mathrm{mL}$ of $2\,\mathrm{M}\,\mathrm{H_2CO_3}$ solution to make an electrolytic buffer. If the same buffer was used in the following electrochemical cell to record a cell potential of $235.3\,\mathrm{mV}$, then the value of $x = \_\_\_\_$ mL (nearest integer).

$\mathrm{Sn(s)} \; \big| \; \mathrm{Sn(OH)_6^{2-}}(0.5\,\mathrm{M}) \; \big| \; \mathrm{HSnO_2^-}(0.05\,\mathrm{M}) \; \big| \; \mathrm{OH^-} \; \big| \; \mathrm{Bi_2O_3(s)} \; \big| \; \mathrm{Bi(s)}$

Consider up to one place of decimal for intermediate calculations.

$\begin{array}{ll}\text{Given:} & E^\circ_{\mathrm{[Sn(OH)_6]^{2-}/HSnO_2^-}} = -0.90\,\mathrm{V} \\[4pt]& E^\circ_{\mathrm{Bi_2O_3/Bi}} = -0.44\,\mathrm{V} \\[4pt]& \mathrm{p}K_a(\mathrm{H_2CO_3}) = 6.11 \\[4pt]& \dfrac{2.303\,RT}{F} = 0.059\,\mathrm{V} \\[6pt]& \text{Antilog}(1.29) = 19.5\end{array}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **78**

**Detailed Solution:**

We have considered
$E^{\circ}_{[Sn(OH)_6]^{2-}/HSnO_2^-} = -0.9 \text{ V}$
$Pt | HSnO_2^-(aq), [Sn(OH)_6]^{2-}(aq), OH^-(aq) | Bi_2O_3(s) | Bi(s) |$
$0.5M \quad 0.05M$
$E^{\circ}_{cell} = +0.9 - 0.44 = 0.46 \text{ V}$
<b>Oxidation Half :</b>
$HSnO_2^- + H_2O + 3OH^- \rightarrow [Sn(OH)_6]^{2-} + 2e^-$
<b>Reduction Half :</b>
$Bi_2O_3 + 3H_2O + 6e^- \rightarrow 2Bi + 6OH^-$
---------------------------------------
$3HSnO_2^-(aq) + Bi_2O_3(s) + 6H_2O + 3OH^-(aq) \rightarrow 3[Sn(OH)_6]^{2-}(aq) + 2Bi(s)$
$E_{cell} = E^{\circ}_{cell} - \dfrac{0.059}{6} \log \dfrac{(0.5)^3}{(0.05)^3 \times [OH^-]^3}$
$0.2353 = 0.46 - \dfrac{0.059}{6} \times 3 \log \left[ \dfrac{10}{[OH^-]} \right]$
$\log \left[ \dfrac{10}{OH^-} \right] = \dfrac{2 \times 0.2247}{0.059} = 7.6$
$1 + pOH = 7.6$
$pOH = 6.6$
$pH = 14 - 6.6 = 7.4$
$pH = pK_{a_1} + \log \dfrac{[HCO_3^-]}{[H_2CO_3]}$
$7.4 = 6.11 + \log \dfrac{5x}{20}$
$1.29 = \log \dfrac{x}{4}$
$\dfrac{x}{4} = 19.5$
$x = 78$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following redox reaction taking place in acidic medium 
 $\mathrm{BH}_{4}^{-}(a q)+\mathrm{ClO}_{3}^{-}(a q) \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{BO}_{3}^{-}(a q)+\mathrm{Cl}^{-}(a q)$ 
 If the Nernst equation for the above balanced reaction is 
 $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}-\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{nF}} \ln \mathrm{Q}$, 
 then the value of $n$ is $\_\_\_\_$. 
 (Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **24**

**Detailed Solution:**

$3BH_4^- + 4ClO_3^- \longrightarrow 4Cl^- + 3H_2BO_3^- + 3H_2O$
n-factor $= 8$
moles $= 3$
$\therefore n = 3 \times 8 = 24$

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Electricity is passed through an acidic solution of $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}$ till all the $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}$ was exhausted, leading to the deposition of 300 mg of Cu metal. However, a current of 600 mA was continued to pass through the same solution for another 28 minutes by keeping the total volume of the solution fixed at 200 mL. The total volume of oxygen evolved at STP during the entire process is $\_\_\_\_$ mL. (Nearest integer) 
 [Given: $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Cu}(\mathrm{s}) \mathrm{E}_{\text {red }}^{\mathrm{o}}=+0.34 \mathrm{~V}$ 
 $\mathrm{O}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})+4 \mathrm{H}^{+}+4 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O} \mathrm{E}_{\text {red }}^{\mathrm{o}}=+1.23 \mathrm{~V}$ 
 Molar mass of $\mathrm{Cu}=63.54 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ 
 Molar mass of $\mathrm{O}_{2}=32 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$  
 Faraday Constant $=96500 \mathrm{C} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ 
 Molar volume at $\mathrm{STP}=22.4 \mathrm{~L}$]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **111**

**Detailed Solution:**

Eq of Cu = Eq of $O_2$
$\dfrac{300 \times 10^{-3} \times 2}{63.54} = n_{O_2} \times 4$
$2.36 \times 10^{-3} = n_{O_2}$
When current is further passed
$n_{O_2} \times 4 = \dfrac{600 \times 28 \times 60}{96500 \times 1000}$
$n_{O_2} = 2.611 \times 10^{-3}$
Total $O_2$ released
$= [10^{-3} \times (2.36 + 2.611)] \times 22400$ ml
$= 111.35$ ml

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In the given electrochemical cell, $\mathrm{Ag}(\mathrm{s})|\mathrm{AgCl}(\mathrm{s})| \mathrm{FeCl}_{2}(\mathrm{aq}), \mathrm{FeCl}_{3}(\mathrm{aq}) \mid \mathrm{Pt}(\mathrm{s})$ at 298 K, the cell potential $\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}\right)$ will increase when : 
 A. Concentration of $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$ is increased. 
 B. Concentration of $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$ is decreased. 
 C. Concentration of $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$ is decreased. 
 D. Concentration of $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$ is increased. 
 E. Concentration of $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$is increased. 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** B Only
- **(B)** A and E Only
- **(C)** C, D and E Only
- **(D)** A and B Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) C, D and E Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

The cell Ag(s)|AgCl(s)|FeCl₂(aq), FeCl₃(aq)|Pt(s) has the reaction:
Ag(s) + Cl⁻ + Fe³⁺ → AgCl(s) + Fe²⁺
Using Nernst equation:
$E_{cell} = E°_{cell} - \frac{0.059}{n}\log Q$ where $Q = \frac{[Fe^{2+}]}{[Fe^{3+}][Cl^-]}$
For $E_{cell}$ to increase, Q must decrease. Analyzing each condition:
A. Increase [Fe²⁺] increases Q, decreases $E_{cell}$ - false
B. Decrease [Fe³⁺] increases Q (smaller denominator), decreases $E_{cell}$ - false
C. Decrease [Fe²⁺] decreases Q, increases $E_{cell}$ - true
D. Increase [Fe³⁺] decreases Q, increases $E_{cell}$ - true
E. Increase [Cl⁻] decreases Q, increases $E_{cell}$ - true
Therefore C, D, and E cause $E_{cell}$ to increase.

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following electrochemical cell : $\mathrm{Pt}\left|\mathrm{O}_{2}(\mathrm{~g})(1 \mathrm{bar})\right| \mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{aq}) \| \mathrm{M}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq}, 1.0 \mathrm{M}) \mid \mathrm{M}(\mathrm{~s})$ The pH above which, oxygen gas would start to evolve at anode is $\_\_\_\_$ (nearest integer). 
 $\left[\begin{array}{ll}\text { Given: } & \mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{o}}{ }_{\mathrm{M}^{2+} / \mathrm{M}}=0.994 \mathrm{~V} \\ & \mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{o}}{ }_{\mathrm{O}_{2} / \mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}}=1.23 \mathrm{~V}\end{array}\right\}$ standard reduction potential $]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

In this electrochemical cell, at the anode oxygen evolution occurs from water oxidation. Using Nernst equation for the O₂/H₂O couple: $E_{anode} = E°_{O_2/H_2O} - \frac{0.059}{4} \log \frac{1}{[H^+]^4} = 1.23 - 0.059 \times pH$

At the cathode: $E_{cathode} = 0.994$ V (standard potential for M²⁺/M reduction)

For oxygen to start evolving, the cell potential becomes zero: $E_{cell} = E_{cathode} - E_{anode} = 0$

$0.994 = 1.23 - 0.059 \times pH$

$0.059 \times pH = 0.236$

$pH = 4.0$

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following reduction processes :
 $\mathrm{Al}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}(\mathrm{s}), \mathrm{E}^{0}=-1.66 \mathrm{~V}$ 
 $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}, \mathrm{E}^{0}=+0.77 \mathrm{~V}$ 
 $\mathrm{Co}^{3+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Co}^{2+}, \mathrm{E}^{0}=+1.81 \mathrm{~V}$ 
 $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cr}(\mathrm{s}), \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.74 \mathrm{~V}$ 
 The tendency to act as reducing agent decreases in the order :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Cr}>\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}>\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cr}>\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}>\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}>\mathrm{Cr}>\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Cr}>\mathrm{Co}^{2+}>\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Cr}>\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}>\mathrm{Co}^{2+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reducing agent strength is determined by oxidation potential (opposite sign of reduction potential). Stronger reducing agents have more negative oxidation potentials (easier to oxidize).
Oxidation potentials:
Al(s) → Al³⁺ has E° = +1.66 V (strongest reducing agent);
Cr(s) → Cr³⁺ has E° = +0.74 V;
Fe²⁺ → Fe³⁺ has E° = -0.77 V;
Co²⁺ → Co³⁺ has E° = -1.81 V (weakest reducing agent).
Order of decreasing reducing agent tendency: Al > Cr > Fe²⁺ > Co²⁺.

</details>

---

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following electrochemical cell at 298 K 
 $\mathrm{Pt}\left|\mathrm{HSnO}_{2}{ }^{-}(\mathrm{aq})\right| \mathrm{Sn}(\mathrm{OH})_{6}{ }^{2-}(\mathrm{aq})\left|\mathrm{OH}^{-}(\mathrm{aq})\right| \mathrm{Bi}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3}(\mathrm{~s}) \mid \mathrm{Bi}(\mathrm{s})$. 
 If the reaction quotient at a given time is $10^{6}$, then the cell EMF ($\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}$) is $\_\_\_\_$ $\times 10^{-1} \mathrm{~V}$ (Nearest integer). 
 Given the standard half-cell reduction potential as 
 $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Bi}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3} / \mathrm{Bi}, \mathrm{OH}^{-}}^{\circ}=-0.44 \mathrm{~V} \text { and } \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Sn}(\mathrm{OH})_{6}^{2-} / \mathrm{HSnO}_{2}^{-}, \mathrm{OH}^{-}}^{\circ}=-0.90 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electrochemical cell has cathode:
$Bi_2O_3 + 3H_2O + 6e^- \rightarrow 2Bi + 6OH^-$ with $E° = -0.44$ V
and anode:
$Sn(OH)_6^{2-} - 2e^- \rightarrow HSnO_2^- + H_2O + OH^-$ with $E° = -0.90$ V.
The standard cell EMF is $E°_{cell} = -0.44 - (-0.90) = 0.46$ V.
Using the Nernst equation with $Q = 10^6$ and $n = 6$ electrons transferred:
$E_{cell} = 0.46 - \frac{0.059}{6} \log(10^6) = 0.46 - \frac{0.059 \times 6}{6} = 0.46 - 0.059 = 0.401$ V.
Expressing as $x \times 10^{-1}$ V:
$0.401 = 4.01 \times 10^{-1}$ V, so $x = 4$ (nearest integer).

</details>

---

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

MX is a sparingly soluble salt that follows the given solubility equilibrium at 298 K. 
 $\mathrm{MX}(\mathrm{s}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{M}^{+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{X}^{-}(\mathrm{aq}) ; \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{sp}}=10^{-10}$ 
 If the standard reduction potential for $\mathrm{M}^{+}(\mathrm{aq}) \xrightarrow{+\mathrm{e}^{-}} \mathrm{M}(\mathrm{s})$ is $\left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{M}^{+} / \mathrm{M}}^{\ominus}\right)=0.79 \mathrm{~V}$, then the value of the standard reduction potential for the metal/metal insoluble salt electrode $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{X}^{-} / \mathrm{MX}(\mathrm{s}) / \mathrm{M}}^{\ominus}$ is $\_\_\_\_$ mV. (nearest integer) 
 [Given : $\frac{2.303 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{F}}=0.059 \mathrm{~V}$ ]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **200**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard reduction potential for a metal/metal insoluble salt electrode is related to the standard reduction potential of the metal ion and the solubility product $K_{sp}$ of the salt.
The half-cell reaction for the $X^-/MX(s)/M$ electrode is: $MX(s) + e^- \rightarrow M(s) + X^-(aq)$.
This can be viewed as a combination of two processes:
1. $MX(s) \rightleftharpoons M^+(aq) + X^-(aq)$ with equilibrium constant $K_{sp} = 10^{-10}$
2. $M^+(aq) + e^- \rightarrow M(s)$ with $E^\ominus_{M^+/M} = 0.79\text{ V}$
The relation between the standard potentials is given by:
$E^\ominus_{X^-/MX/M} = E^\ominus_{M^+/M} + \frac{2.303RT}{nF} \log_{10} K_{sp}$
Given $n = 1$ and $\frac{2.303RT}{F} = 0.059\text{ V}$:
$E^\ominus_{X^-/MX/M} = 0.79 + 0.059 \log_{10}(10^{-10})$
$E^\ominus_{X^-/MX/M} = 0.79 + 0.059(-10)$
$E^\ominus_{X^-/MX/M} = 0.79 - 0.59 = 0.20\text{ V}$
To convert the value into mV:
$0.20\text{ V} = 0.20 \times 1000\text{ mV} = 200\text{ mV}$

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For a closed circuit Daniell cell, which of the following plots is the accurate one at a given temperature?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img style="width:200px;"  src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_o_67_1_1.png">
- **(B)** <img style="width:200px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_o_67_2_1.png">
- **(C)** <img style="width:200px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_o_67_3_1.png">
- **(D)** <img style="width:200px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_o_67_4_1_v2.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img style="width:200px;"  src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_o_67_1_1.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard cell potential $E^\circ_{cell}$ is a thermodynamic property defined for a cell under standard conditions (concentrations of all species at $1\text{ M}$, pressure at $1\text{ bar}$, and a specific temperature).
For a Daniell cell, the reaction is $Zn(s) + Cu^{2+}(aq) \rightarrow Zn^{2+}(aq) + Cu(s)$.
The standard cell potential is given by $E^\circ_{cell} = E^\circ_{Cu^{2+}/Cu} - E^\circ_{Zn^{2+}/Zn}$.
Since $E^\circ_{cell}$ depends only on the nature of the electrodes, the nature of the electrolytes, and the temperature, it remains constant over time as long as the temperature is constant.
While the actual cell potential $E_{cell}$ changes as the reaction proceeds (due to changes in concentration according to the Nernst equation), the standard cell potential $E^\circ_{cell}$ is a constant value.
Therefore, the plot of $E^\circ_{cell}$ versus time is a horizontal line parallel to the time axis.

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (8 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following half cell reaction
$\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}(\mathrm{aq})+6 \mathrm{e}^{-}+14 \mathrm{H}^{+}(\mathrm{aq})$ $\rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+7 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})$
The reaction was conducted with the ratio of $\frac{\left[\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}\right]}=10^{-6}$. The pH value at which the EMF of the half cell will become zero is ________. (nearest integer value)
[Given : standard half cell reduction potential
$\left.\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}, \mathrm{H}^{+} / \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}}^{\mathrm{o}}=1.33 \mathrm{~V}, \frac{2.303 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{~F}}=0.059 \mathrm{~V}\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_{7 \text { (aq) }}^{-2}+14 \mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+6 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Cr}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+3}+7 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}_{(\ell)} \\ & \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{R}}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{R}}^0-\frac{0.059}{6} \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cr}^{+3}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{-2}\right]\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]^{14}} \\ & 0=1.33-\frac{0.059}{6} \log \frac{10^{-6}}{\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]^{14}} \\ & \frac{1.33 \times 6}{0.059}=\log \frac{10^{-6}}{[\mathrm{H}]^{14}} \\ & 135.254=-6-14 \log \left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right] \\ & 141.254=14 \mathrm{pH} \\ & \mathrm{pH}=\frac{141.254}{14}=10.08\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given below are two statements :
1 M aqueous solution of each of $\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{NO}_3\right)_2, \mathrm{AgNO}_3$, $\mathrm{Hg}_2\left(\mathrm{NO}_3\right)_2 ; \mathrm{Mg}\left(\mathrm{NO}_3\right)_2$ are electrolysed using inert electrodes,
$\begin{aligned} & \text { Given : } \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}}^\theta=0.80 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Hg}_2^{2+} / \mathrm{Hg}}^\theta=0.79 \mathrm{~V}, \\ & \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} / \mathrm{Cu}}^\theta=0.24 \mathrm{~V} \text { and } \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} / \mathrm{Mg}}^\theta=-2.37 \mathrm{~V}\end{aligned}$
Statement (I) : With increasing voltage, the sequence of deposition of metals on the cathode will be $\mathrm{Ag}, \mathrm{Hg}$ and Cu

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

$1$ Faraday electricity was passed through $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(1.5$ $\mathrm{M}, 1 \mathrm{~L}) / \mathrm{Cu}$ and $0.1$ Faraday was passed through $\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(0.2 \mathrm{M}, 1 \mathrm{~L}) / \mathrm{Ag}$ electrolytic cells. After this the two cells were connected as shown below to make an electrochemical cell. The emf of the cell thus formed at 298 K is-
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Qj5Oh23y-5QPrWcRlu57rCfE6Ko6J2pn_RhCR7ODRrY.original.fullsize.png"/>
<strong>Given:</strong> $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} / \mathrm{Cu}}^{\mathrm{o}}=0.34 \mathrm{~V}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}}^0=0.8 \mathrm{~V}$
$\frac{2.303 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{F}}=0.06 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **400**

**Detailed Solution:**

* $\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}$
$\begin{aligned}& (1 \text { faraday }=\text { charge on } 1 \text { mole electron }) \\& \mathrm{t}=0 \quad 1.5 \quad 1 \mathrm{~mole} \\& \mathrm{t}=\mathrm{t} \quad 1 \quad-\quad 0.5 \text { mole }\end{aligned}$
$\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$ after electrolysis
* $\mathrm{Ag} \oplus+\mathrm{e}^{-} \quad \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ag}$
$\begin{array}{lll}\mathrm{t}=0 & 0.2 & 0.1 \text { mole } \\ \mathrm{t}=\mathrm{t} & 0.1 & - \qquad -\end{array}$
$\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=0.1 \mathrm{M}$ after electrolysis
Cell $\quad \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+} \rightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
reaction
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{E}=\mathrm{E}^{\circ}-\frac{0.06}{\mathrm{n}} \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^2} \\ & \mathrm{E}=(0.8-0.34)-\frac{0.06}{2} \log \frac{1}{(0.1)^2}=0.4 \mathrm{~V}\end{aligned}$
Correct answer $=400 \mathrm{mV}$

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard cell potential $\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\ominus}\right)$ of a fuel cell based on the oxidation of methanol in air that has been used to power television relay station is measured as 1.21 V. The standard half cell reduction potential for $\mathrm{O}_2\left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{O}_2 / \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}}^{\mathrm{O}}\right)$ is 1.229 V.
Choose the correct statement:

**Options:**

- **(A)** The standard half cell reduction potential for the reduction of $\mathrm{CO}_2\left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{CO}_2 / \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}}^0\right)$ is 19 mV
- **(B)** Oxygen is formed at the anode.
- **(C)** Reactants are fed at one go to each electrode.
- **(D)** Reduction of methanol takes place at the cathode.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) The standard half cell reduction potential for the reduction of $\mathrm{CO}_2\left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{CO}_2 / \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}}^0\right)$ is 19 mV**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
\because \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}} & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cathode }}^{\mathrm{o}}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {Anode }}^{\mathrm{o}} \\
1.21 & =1.229-\mathrm{E}_{\text {Anode }}^{\mathrm{o}}
\end{aligned}$
$\because$ Fuel cell involves oxidation of methanol which will occur at anode and reduction of $\mathrm{O}_2$ will occur at cathode.

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following electrochemical cell at standard condition.
\(\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Au}(\mathrm{s})\left|\mathrm{QH}_2, \mathrm{Q}\right| \mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{X}(0.01 \mathrm{M})| | \mathrm{Ag}^{+}(1 \mathrm{M}) \mid \mathrm{Ag}(\mathrm{s}) \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=+0.4 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}\)
The couple \(\mathrm{QH}_2 / \mathrm{Q}\) represents quinhydrone electrode, the half cell reaction is given below
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/o-CLozdN3jEnUeNZlJ1pEsvN_QRpgugJTyhaLmZDMFk.original.fullsize.png"/>
The \(\mathrm{pK}_{\mathrm{b}}\) value of the ammonium halide salt \(\left(\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{X}\right)\) used here is _________. (nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

\(\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{QH}_2+2 \mathrm{Ag}^{+} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Ag}+\mathrm{Q}+2 \mathrm{H}^{+} \\
& \mathrm{E}=\mathrm{E}^{\circ}-\frac{0.06}{2} \log \left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]^2 \\
& \mathrm{E}=\mathrm{E}^{\circ}-0.06 \times \log \left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right] \\
& \mathrm{pH}=-\log \left(\mathrm{H}^{+}\right)=\frac{\mathrm{E}-\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{o}}}{0.06}=\frac{0.4-0.1}{0.06} \\
& =\frac{0.3}{0.06}=5
\end{aligned}\)
\(\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{pH}+\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{X}=7-\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{pK}_{\mathrm{b}}-\frac{1}{2} \log \mathrm{C} \\ & 5=7-\frac{1}{2} \times \mathrm{pK}_{\mathrm{b}}-\frac{1}{2} \log \left(10^{-2}\right) \\ & \mathrm{pK}_{\mathrm{b}}=6\end{aligned}\)

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For a \(\mathrm{Mg}\left|\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(\mathrm{aq})\right| \mathrm{Ag}\) the correct Nernst Equation is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{2 \mathrm{~F}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}\)
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}+\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{2 \mathrm{~F}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}\)
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{2 \mathrm{~F}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}\)
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{2 \mathrm{~F}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}+\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{2 \mathrm{~F}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{lll}\text { Cathode } & \left(\mathrm{Ag}+(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{e}^{-}\right. & \rightarrow \mathrm{Ag}) \times 2 \\ \text { Anode } & \mathrm{Mg} & \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})+2 \mathrm{e}^{-}\end{array}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5fehv2XCOqyVLYn-Jebb22QyBHpkAqQVDjHWF23wOqg.original.fullsize.png">

By Nernst equation
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\circ}-\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{nF}} \ln \mathrm{Q} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\circ}-\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{nF}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^2} \\
& =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}+\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{2 \mathrm{~F}} \ln \frac{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}\right]}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Electrolysis of 600 mL aqueous solution of NaCl for 5 min changes the pH of the solution to 12 . The current in Amperes used for the given electrolysis is ____. (Nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Electrolysis of NaCl is
$\mathrm{NaCl}+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{aq}) \rightarrow \mathrm{NaOH}(\mathrm{aq})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})$

Since during electrolysis pH changes to 12
So $\left[\mathrm{OH}^{\ominus}\right]=10^{-2}$ and $\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]=10^{-12}$
So by Faraday law
Gram amount of substance deposited $=$ Amount of electricity passed


$\begin{aligned} & 10^{-2} \times \frac{600}{1000} \times 96500=\mathrm{I} \times \mathrm{t} \\ & \frac{10^{-2} \times 600}{1000} \times 96500=\mathrm{I} \times 5 \times 60 \\ & \mathrm{I}=\frac{10^{-2} \times 600 \times 96500}{1000 \times 5 \times 60} \\ & \mathrm{I}=1.93 \text { ampere } \\ & \text { So }, I=2 \text { ampere (nearest integer) }\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q23. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Based on the data given below :
$\begin{array}{ll}\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-} / \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}}^{\circ}=1.33 \mathrm{~V} & \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cl}_2 / \mathrm{Cl}^{(-)}}^{\circ}=1.36 \mathrm{~V} \\ \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-} / \mathrm{Mn}^{2+}}^{\circ}=1.51 \mathrm{~V} & \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}^{3+} / \mathrm{Cr}}^{\circ}=-0.74 \mathrm{~V}\end{array}$

the strongest reducing agent is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Cr
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Cr**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{Of}_4^{\circ} / \mathrm{ca}^8}^0=1.33 \mathrm{~V}_{\mathrm{C}_2 / \mathrm{Cr}}^{\circ}=1.36 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{MnO}_4 / \mathrm{Mm}^2}^{\circ}=1.51 \mathrm{~V} \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}^2 / \mathrm{Cr}}^0=-0.74 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$
The species which has the most negative value of standard reduction potential will be the strongest reducing agent. Since $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+} / \mathrm{Cr}$ has SRP value of $-0.74 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{Cr}$ is the strongest reducing agent.

### Q24. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the given cell
$\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+\mathrm{Ag}^{+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{3+}+\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
The standard cell potential of the above reaction is Given:
$\begin{array}{lr}
\mathrm{Ag}^{+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ag} & \mathrm{E}^\theta=\mathrm{xV} \\
\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe} & \mathrm{E}^\theta=\mathrm{yV} \\
\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe} & \mathrm{E}^\theta=\mathrm{zV}
\end{array}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y-z$
- **(B)** $x+2 y$
- **(C)** $x+2 y-3 z$
- **(D)** $y-2 x$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x+2 y-3 z$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(\mathrm{aq}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{Ag}(\mathrm{s})$



<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/OylHHZ3rje-XAFBAvTr57B7kOZBmadWhQux-RssOOUo.original.fullsize.png">


$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{G}_3^0=\Delta \mathrm{G}_1^0+\Delta \mathrm{G}_2^0 \\
& -3 \mathrm{~F}(-\mathrm{z})=-2 \mathrm{~F}(-\mathrm{y})+\Delta \mathrm{G}_2{ }^0 \\
& \Delta \mathrm{G}_2^0=3 \mathrm{Fz}-2 \mathrm{Fy}
\end{aligned}$

Also $\Delta \mathrm{G}_2^0=-\mathrm{nFE}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{+2} / \mathrm{Fe}^{t^{43}}}^0$
$\begin{aligned}
& 3 \mathrm{Fz}-2 \mathrm{Fy}=-1 \mathrm{~F}\left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{t^2} / \mathrm{Fe}^{43}}^0\right) \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{2+2} / \mathrm{Fe}^{t^3}}^0=2 \mathrm{y}-3 \mathrm{z}
\end{aligned}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\text {Cell }}^0$ for reaction will be
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}}^0+\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{t^2 / \mathrm{Fe}}}^{03} \\
& =\mathrm{x}+2 \mathrm{y}-3 \mathrm{z}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Standard electrode potentials for a few half cells are mentioned below :
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} / \mathrm{Cu}}^{\circ}=0.34 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Zn}^{2+} / \mathrm{Zn}}^{\circ}=-0.76 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ}=0.80 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} / \mathrm{Mg}}^{\circ}=-2.37 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$
Which one of the following cells gives the most negative value of $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Zn}\left|\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Ag}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Zn}\left|\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right|\left|\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Mg}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Ag}\left|\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right|\left|\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Mg}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cu}\left|\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M}) \| \mathrm{Ag}^{+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Ag}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Zn}\left|\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Ag}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{gathered}\because \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-\mathrm{nFE}^{\circ} \\ \text { Option }(1) \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=0.8+0.76 \\ =1.56 \mathrm{~V} \\ \therefore \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-2 \times \mathrm{F} \times 1.56 \\ =-3.12 \mathrm{~V} \\ \text { Option (2) } \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-2.37+0.76 \\ =-1.61 \mathrm{~V} \\ \therefore \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-2 \times \mathrm{F} \times(-1.61) \\ =+3.22 \mathrm{~V} \\ \text { Option (3) } \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-2.37-0.8 \\ =-3.17 \mathrm{~V} \\ \left.\begin{array}{c}\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-2\end{array}\right) \times \mathrm{F} \times(-3.17) \\ =+6.34 \\ \text { Option }(4) \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=0.8-0.34 \\ =0.46 \mathrm{~V} \\ \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-2 \times \mathrm{F} \times 0.46 \\ =-0.92 \mathrm{~V}\end{gathered}$

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$\mathrm{FeO}_4^{2-} \xrightarrow{+2.0 \mathrm{v}} \mathrm{Fe}^{3+} \xrightarrow{0.8 \mathrm{v}} \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} \xrightarrow{-0.5 \mathrm{v}} \mathrm{Fe}^0$
In the above diagram, the standard electrode potentials are given in volts (over the arrow).
The value of $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{FeO}_4^{2-} / \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}}^{\mathrm{O}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2.1 V
- **(B)** 1 .7 V
- **(C)** 1 .4 V
- **(D)** 1 .2 V

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1 .7 V**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/E0jLLKybOrSiTMJIfYil4S-gQbagWzv71FBDElK14MQ.original.fullsize.png">


$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{G}_4^{\mathrm{o}}=\Delta \mathrm{G}_1^{\mathrm{o}}+\Delta \mathrm{G}_2^{\mathrm{o}} \\ \Rightarrow & -\mathrm{n}_4 \mathrm{FE}_4^{\mathrm{o}}=-\mathrm{n}_1 \mathrm{FE}_1^0-\mathrm{n}_2 \mathrm{FE}_2^{\mathrm{o}} \\ \Rightarrow & +4 \mathrm{E}_4^{\mathrm{o}}=3 \times 2+(1 \times 0.8) \\ \Rightarrow & \mathrm{E}_4^{\mathrm{o}}=\frac{6.8}{4} \mathrm{~V} \\ \Rightarrow & \mathrm{E}_4^{\mathrm{o}}=1.7 \mathrm{~V}\end{aligned}$

### Q27. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

A solution of aluminium chloride is electrolysed for 30 minutes using a current of 2 A . The amount of the aluminium deposited at the cathode is
[Given : molar mass of aluminium and chlorine are $27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ and $35.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively. Faraday constant $\left.=96500 \mathrm{C} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.660 g
- **(B)** 0.336 g
- **(C)** 0.441 g
- **(D)** 1.007 g

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 0.336 g**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Al}^{1++}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}$
$\begin{aligned}
\text { Moles of electron } & =\frac{2 \times 30 \times 60}{96500} \\
& =\frac{36}{965}
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}
\text { Moles of } \mathrm{Al} & =\frac{36}{3 \times 965} \\
& =\frac{12}{965}
\end{aligned}$
$\text { Mass of } \mathrm{Al}=\frac{12}{965} \times 27$
$=0.336 \mathrm{gm}$

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard reduction potentials at \(298 \mathrm{~K}\) for the following half cells are given below :
\(\begin{array}{ll}
\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}+14 \mathrm{H}^{+}+6 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}+7 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}, \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=1.33 \mathrm{~V} \\
\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe} \qquad \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.04 \mathrm{~V} \\
\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ni} \qquad \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.25 \mathrm{~V} \\
\mathrm{Ag}^{+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ag} \quad\qquad \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=0.80 \mathrm{~V} \\
\mathrm{Au}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Au} \qquad \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=1.40 \mathrm{~V}
\end{array}\)
Consider the given electrochemical reactions,
The number of metal(s) which will be oxidized be \(\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}\), in aqueous solution is ______

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Fe}, \mathrm{Ni}, \mathrm{Ag}$ will be oxidized due to lower S.R.P.

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The emf of cell Tl \(\left|\underset{(0.001 \mathrm{M})}{\mathrm{Tl}^{+}}\right|\left|\underset{(0.01 \mathrm{M})}{\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}}\right| \mathrm{Cu}\) is \(0.83 \mathrm{~V}\) at \(298 \mathrm{~K}\). It could be increased by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** decreasing concentration of both \(\mathrm{Tl}^{+}\) and \(\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\) ions
- **(B)** increasing concentration of \(\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\) ions
- **(C)** increasing concentration of \(\mathrm{T1}^{+}\)ions
- **(D)** increasing concentration of both \(\mathrm{Tl}^{+}\) and \(\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\) ions

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) increasing concentration of \(\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\) ions**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll} 
\text{Anodic Reaction} & {\left[\mathrm{T} \ell_{(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{T}^{+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+\mathrm{e}^{-}\right] 2} \\
\text{Cathodic Reaction} & \mathrm{Cu}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\
\hline \text{Overall Redox Reaction} & 2 \mathrm{~T} \ell_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{~T} \ell_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}
\end{array}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^o-\frac{0.0591}{2} \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{T} \ell^{+}\right]^2}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}\right]}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}$ increases by increasing concentration of $\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}\right]$ ions.

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

How can an electrochemical cell be converted into an electrolytic cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Applying an external opposite potential lower than \(\mathrm{E}^0\) cell.
- **(B)** Reversing the flow of ions in salt bridge.
- **(C)** Applying an external opposite potential greater than \(\mathrm{E}^0\) cell-
- **(D)** Exchanging the electrodes at anode and cathode.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Applying an external opposite potential greater than \(\mathrm{E}^0\) cell-**

**Detailed Solution:**

Applied external potential should be greater than $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0$ in opposite direction.

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the electro chemical cell
\(\begin{gathered}
\text { If } \mathrm{E}_{\left(\mathrm{M}^{2+} / \mathrm{M}\right)}^0=0.46 \mathrm{~V} \text { and } \mathrm{E}_{\left(\mathrm{x} / \mathrm{X}^{2-}\right)}^0=0.34 \mathrm{~V} \text {. }
\end{gathered}\)
Which of the following is correct?

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{M}+\mathrm{X} \rightarrow \mathrm{M}^{2+}+\mathrm{X}^{2-}\) is a spontaneous reaction
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=0.80 \mathrm{~V}\)
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=-0.80 \mathrm{~V}\)
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{M}^{2+}+\mathrm{X}^{2-} \rightarrow \mathrm{M}+\mathrm{X}\) is a spontaneous reaction

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) \(\mathrm{M}^{2+}+\mathrm{X}^{2-} \rightarrow \mathrm{M}+\mathrm{X}\) is a spontaneous reaction**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{M} \mid \mathrm{M}^{+2} \| \mathrm{X} / \mathrm{X}^{2-} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\mathrm{o}}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{M} / \mathrm{M}^{+2}}^{\mathrm{o}}+\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{X} / \mathrm{X}^{-2}}^{\mathrm{o}} \\
& =-0.46+0.34=-0.12 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$
As $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}$ is negative so anode becomes cathode and cathode become anode. Spontaneous reaction will be $\mathrm{M}^{+2}+\mathrm{X}^{2-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{M}+\mathrm{X}$

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The quantity of silver deposited when one coulomb charge is passed through \(\mathrm{AgNO}_3\) solution :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(1 \mathrm{~g}\) of silver
- **(B)** 1 electrochemical equivalent of silver
- **(C)** 1 chemical equivalent of silver
- **(D)** \(0.1 \mathrm{~g}\) atom of silver

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1 electrochemical equivalent of silver**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{W}=\mathrm{ZIt} \\ & \mathrm{W}=\mathrm{ZQ} \\ & \mathrm{Q}=\frac{\mathrm{W}}{\mathrm{Z}} \\ & \mathrm{W}=\mathrm{ZQ}=\text { (electrochemical equivalent) }\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The amount of electricity in Coulomb required for the oxidation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

### Q35. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The Nernst equation for the given cell reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>→</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>°</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mi>log</mi><mo> </mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>03</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>009</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

One Faraday of electricity liberates <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>–</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> gram atom of copper from copper sulphate, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is______.

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Reduction potential of ions are given below:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo></mrow><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>  </mo><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>  </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>19</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo> </mo><mo>;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>  </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>74</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>The correct order of their oxidising power is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p style="margin-top:0px; margin-bottom:0px; text-align:start; -webkit-text-stroke-width:0px"><span style="font-size:16px"><span style="box-sizing:border-box"><span style="-webkit-tap-highlight-color:transparent"><span style="color:#333333"><span style="font-family:GothamSSm"><span style="font-style:normal"><span style="font-variant-ligatures:normal"><span style="font-variant-caps:normal"><span style="font-weight:400"><span style="letter-spacing:normal"><span style="orphans:2"><span style="text-transform:none"><span style="widows:2"><span style="word-spacing:0px"><span style="white-space:normal"><span style="text-decoration-thickness:initial"><span style="text-decoration-style:initial"><span style="text-decoration-color:initial"><span style="background-color:null;">The reduction potential of the substance is the ability of the substance to be reduced. So as the reduction potential increases, the reducing ability of the substance increases. It means the oxidising power of the substance (The ability of the substance to make the other substance to lose the electrons increases, which nothing but oxidising power) increases.</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></p><p style="margin-top:0px; margin-bottom:0px; text-align:start; -webkit-text-stroke-width:0px"><span style="font-size:16px"><span style="box-sizing:border-box"><span style="-webkit-tap-highlight-color:transparent"><span style="color:#333333"><span style="font-family:GothamSSm"><span style="font-style:normal"><span style="font-variant-ligatures:normal"><span style="font-variant-caps:normal"><span style="font-weight:400"><span style="letter-spacing:normal"><span style="orphans:2"><span style="text-transform:none"><span style="widows:2"><span style="word-spacing:0px"><span style="white-space:normal"><span style="text-decoration-thickness:initial"><span style="text-decoration-style:initial"><span style="text-decoration-color:initial"><span style="background-color:null;">So the decreasing order of oxidising power is </span><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>BrO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>IO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math><span style="background-color:null;">.</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></p><p><span style="font-size:16px; text-align:start; -webkit-text-stroke-width:0px"><span style="color:#333333"><span style="font-family:GothamSSm"><span style="font-style:normal"><span style="font-variant-ligatures:normal"><span style="font-variant-caps:normal"><span style="font-weight:400"><span style="letter-spacing:normal"><span style="orphans:2"><span style="text-transform:none"><span style="widows:2"><span style="word-spacing:0px"><span style="white-space-collapse: collapse;"><span style="text-decoration-thickness: initial;"><span style="text-decoration-style: initial;"><span style="text-decoration-color: initial;"><span style="display: inline !important;"><span style="float: none;">Hence option <span style="text-wrap: nowrap;">B</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span style="font-size:16px; text-align:start; -webkit-text-stroke-width:0px"><span style="color:#333333"><span style="font-family:GothamSSm"><span style="font-style:normal"><span style="font-variant-ligatures:normal"><span style="font-variant-caps:normal"><span style="font-weight:400"><span style="letter-spacing:normal"><span style="orphans:2"><span style="text-transform:none"><span style="widows:2"><span style="word-spacing:0px"><span style="white-space:normal"><span style="text-decoration-thickness:initial"><span style="text-decoration-style:initial"><span style="text-decoration-color:initial"><span style="display:inline !important"><span style="float:none"><span style="background-color:null;"> is correct.</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></p><p> </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A constant current was passed through a solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>AuCl</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> ion between gold electrodes. After a period of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn></math> minutes, the increase in mass of cathode was <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>314</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>. The total charge passed through the solution is ___<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>. (Given atomic mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Au</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>197</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The half-cell reaction is,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Au</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>−</mo></msup><mo> </mo><mo>→</mo><mo> </mo><mi>Au</mi></math></p><p>Given W = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>314</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p><p>Atomic mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Au</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>197</mn></math></p><p>When one Faraday of charge is passed through a circuit, then one equivalent of charge is deposited at the respective electrode.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo> </mo><mi>charge</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>314</mn></mrow><mfrac><mn>197</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The mass of zinc produced by the electrolysis of zinc sulphate solution with a steady current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>015</mn><mo> </mo><mi>A</mi></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn></math> minutes is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>_</mo><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>. (Atomic mass of zinc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>65</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>amu</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **46**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, t = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo> </mo></math>min</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>⟶</mo><mi>Zn</mi></math></p><p>Quantity of charge passed (Q) =Current in amperes ×Time in seconds.
=<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>015</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>×</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p>Gram equivalent mass of zinc = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>65</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn></math>g
Now,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>96500</mn></math> C of charge deposit zinc =<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn></math> g
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> C charge will deposit zinc = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>96500</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi>gm</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The hydrogen electrode is dipped in a solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>pH</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The potential of the electrode will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo></math>______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> Round off the answer to the nearest integer.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **18**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,
pH =<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo>-</mo><mo> </mo><mi>log</mi><mo> </mo><mo>[</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>]</mo><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>[</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>]</mo><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></math></p><p>The half cell reaction is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>→</mo><msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></msub></math></p><p>We know,</p><p>the Nernst equation is
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>red</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mi>red</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn><mo> </mo><mi>log</mi><mo> </mo><mo>[</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>]</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi>log</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn><mo> </mo><mo>×</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mo> </mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>177</mn><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The mass of silver (Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>108</mn><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) displaced by a quantity of electricity which displaces <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5600</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> at S.T.P. will be _____ g.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **108**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>108</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Equal equivalents of substances deposited or liberated when some quantity of electricity passed through circuite in the electrolysis cell.</p><p>Equivalents of silver deposited= Equivalents of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> liberated.
Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> gm silver displaced,
(Molar volume of gas at STP <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> )</p><p>Number equivalents of a substance = The number of moles of substance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>×</mo></math> Valence factor of that substance
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>108</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>22</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>108</mn><mo> </mo><mi>gm</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, the standard reduction potential for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cu</mi></math> electrode is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>sp</mi></msub><mi>Cu</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></math> Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> The reduction potential at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>pH</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math> for the above couple is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>)</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. The value of x is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the reaction:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math>

The reduction potential is

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#959;</mi></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>RT</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></mfrac></math>

From the given data <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>pH</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>14</mn></math> and

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>sp</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.0</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>10</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></math>, we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>,</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>

     <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi>sp</mi></msub><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.0</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>10</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.0</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>10</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>

            <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=0.34</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.059</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>log</mi><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.0</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>10</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.34</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.059</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>59</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>A metal surface of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> area has to be coated with nickel layer of thickness <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mm</mi><mo>.</mo></math> A current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> was passed through a solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8216;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#8217;</mo></math> seconds to coat the desired layer. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is _________. (Nearest integer)</p><p>(<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#961;Ni</mi></math> (density of Nickel) is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mL</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>Molar mass of Nickel is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Cmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **16**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>One equivalent of substance deposited for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>96500</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>(one Faraday).</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math> Columbus of charge can be utilised to get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of substance.</p><p>Millimoles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> coated</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mn>965</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>965</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>600</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>600</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mn>965</mn></mfrac><mi>mg</mi></math></p><p>Volume<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>60</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mn>965</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1608</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>161</mn></math></p>

</details>
