### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The number of correct statements from the following is...........
A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub></math> is an intensive parameter
B. A negative <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></math> means that the redox couple is a stronger reducing agent than the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> couple.
C. The amount of electricity required for oxidation or reduction depends on the stoichiometry of the electrode reaction.
D. The amount of chemical reaction which occurs at any electrode during electrolysis by a current is proportional to the quantity of electricity passed through the electrolyte.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>An intensive property is a bulk property, meaning that it is a physical property of a system that does not depend on the system size or the amount of material in the system.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><msub><mi>nFE</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8203;</mo></math></p><p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi></math> in the above equation is an extensive property, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub></math> is an intensive property. A negative <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></math>means that the redox couple is a stronger reducing agent than the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>couple. The amount of electricity required for oxidation or reduction depends on the stoichiometry of the electrode reaction. The amount of chemical reaction which occurs at any electrode during electrolysis by a current is proportional to the quantity of electricity passed through the electrolyte.</p>

</details>

---

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>In an electrochemical reaction of lead, at standard temperature, if  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mtext>&#160;Volt&#160;&#160;</mtext></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Volt</mi><mo>,</mo></math>then the value of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>  is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>xn</mi></math>. The value of x is _______. (Nearest integer)</p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The relation between Gibbs Free energy and emf of the cell can be related as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>nFE</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub></math></p><p>The electrode reactions can be written as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow></mrow></mover><mi>Pb</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow></mrow></mover><mi>Pb</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow></mrow></mover><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p> </p>

### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The reaction occurs in which of the given galvanic cell?</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mo>|</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>HCl</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>sol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi><mo>|</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>KCl</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>sol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>|</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mo>|</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>HCl</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>sol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>sol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mo>|</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><msup><mrow/><mn>2</mn></msup></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>KCl</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>sol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mo>|</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>HCl</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>sol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Oxidation is loss of electron and in a galvanic cell it occurs at anode. Reduction is gain of electron and in a galvanic cell it occurs at cathode.</p><p>At anode :<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#10230;</mo><munder><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></munder><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>At cathode <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><munder><mi>AgCl</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></munder><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo><munder><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mtext>&#160;(aq)&#160;</mtext></munder></math></p><p>Overall reaction</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#10230;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Cell representation :</p><p>Anode / Anode electrolyte || Cathodic electrolyte / Cathode.</p><p>The cell representation for the above cell will be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced><mi>HCl</mi><mfenced><msup><mtext>&#160;soln&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msup></mfenced><mo>|</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>cathode</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>reduction</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>anode</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>oxidation</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></math> values when metal acts as anode and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> reaction is cathodic reaction.</p><p>For Vanadium metal, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>19</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>16</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>For Iron metal, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>04</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>For silver metal, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>17</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>For Gold metal, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>140</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>17</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>For electrodes having oxidation potential greater than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> can be oxidised</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The product, which is not obtained during the electrolysis of brine solution is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The aqueous solution of sodium chloride is known as brine solution. On electrolysis of brine solution, the following reactions are observed.</p><p>Oxidation reaction takes place at anode and reduction reaction takes place at cathode. The reaction</p><p>Anode: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8861;</mo></msup></math></p><p>Cathode: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8861;</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#8861;</mo></msup></math></p><p>Hydrogen and oxygen are electrode products. The remaining solution contain <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> is not obtained</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q49. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The standard electrode potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> in aqueous solution does not depend on</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Hydration of a gaseous metal ion
- **(B)** <p>Sublimation of a solid metal</p>
- **(C)** Ionisation of a solid metal atom
- **(D)** Ionisation of a gaseous metal atom

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Ionisation of a solid metal atom**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The stability of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> ions in aqueous medium depends on three factors: (i) Enthalpy of atomisation (ii) First ionisation enthalpies of isolated gaseous atom (iii) Hydration enthalpy. </p><p>An element in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> state in aqueous medium is stabler if the electrode potential ( <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>) value of more negative. Down the group, the tendency to form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> ion increases. In this process, ionisation of solid metal is not involved in this process.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi close="]"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>282</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced ### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The logarithm of equilibrium constant for the reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pd</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8652;</mo><msubsup><mi>PdCl</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo></math> is
(Nearest integer)</p><p>Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>Pd</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8652;</mo><mi>Pd</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>83</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>PdCl</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8652;</mo><mi>Pd</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>Using gibbs free energy equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>RT</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>lnK</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi>nFE</mi><mtext>cell&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>RT</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>log</mi><mn>10</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>log</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pd</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8652;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pd</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>cathode,&#160;reduction&#160;</mtext><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>83</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pd</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi>PdCl</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>Net Reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Pd</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8652;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><msub><mi>PdCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>cell&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>cathode,reduction</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>anode, oxidation&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Also <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Using equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>&amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo></math>, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>Cell</mtext><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi </p>

### Q52. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Which one of the following statements is correct for electrolysis of brine solution?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is formed at cathode
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is formed at cathode
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is formed at anode
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>is formed at cathode

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>is formed at cathode**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Brine solution is the concentrated solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> in water.</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msubsup><mi>Na</mi><mtext>(aq)&#160;</mtext><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>Cl</mi><mtext>(aq)&#160;</mtext><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math></p><p>The cathode will attract the sodium ions from the sodium chloride and hydrogen ions from the water. The hydrogen ions gain 2 electrons and are reduced to form hydrogen molecules.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>The anode will attract the hydroxide ions from the water and chlorine ions from sodium chloride. The chlorine ions lose 2 electrons and get oxidised.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaOH</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The electrode potential of the following half cell at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8214;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> (Nearest integer)</p><p>Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8739;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **275**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The net cell reaction can be written as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cathode</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>anode</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math>(Both are SRP values)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>Cell</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>Now, the Nernst equation is,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math>The number of electrons involved in the net reaction.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>Cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>03</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>275</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Consider the cell</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Pt</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></msub><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>atm</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>aq</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>|</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>Pt</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>When the potential of the cell is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>712</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mo>/</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></math> is
(Nearest integer)
Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><menclose notation="right"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></menclose><mi>Pt</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>771</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given cell reaction:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Pt</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></msub><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>atm</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>aq</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>|</mo><mo>|</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>Pt</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>at anode(oxidation) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#10230;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>At cathode(reduction) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>Fe</mi><mi>aq</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#10230;</mo><msubsup><mi>Fe</mi><mi>aq</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cathode</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>anode</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>|</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mrow><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8739;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>771</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>Thus, using Nernst equation,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>712</mn><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>771</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>log</mi><mfrac><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><menclose notation="box"><mfrac><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></menclose></math></p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The standard electrode potential (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>) for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cr</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Mn</mi></math> &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, respectively. The metal ions which can liberate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> from a dilute acid are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8861;</mo></msup></math> values for the redox couple (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>) shows that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ions are the strongest oxidising agents in aqueous solution.</p><p>The ions <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ti</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> are strong reducing agents and will liberate hydrogen from a dilute acid.</p><p>So, the Correct answer is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The amount of charge in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> (Faraday) required to obtain one mole of iron from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is ______. (Round off the answer to the nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is a mixed oxide(<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mover><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>)</p><p>The net change in oxidation can be calculated as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mover><mn>3</mn><mi>Fe</mi></math></p><p>For 3 moles of formation of iron, 8 moles of electrons are involved.</p><p>One mole of electrons charge is called one Faraday.</p><p>Charge for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The cell potential for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8214;</mo><msup><mi>Sn</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>Sn</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>801</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The reaction quotient for the above reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The number of electrons involved in the given electrochemical cell reaction is _____ .
(Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8739;</mo><mi>Zn</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>763</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Sn</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi>Sn</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>008</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>303</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi>nF</mi></mfrac><mi>logQ</mi></math></p><p>Here,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>801</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>Cathode</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>Anode</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mfenced><mrow><mi>Both</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>are</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>SRP</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>values</mi></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>008</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>763</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>771</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>801</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>771</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mi>log</mi><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The quantity of electricity in Faraday needed to reduce <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>1 faraday of charge is the magnitude of charge of one mole of electrons.</p><p>According to the given reaction: 
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#10230;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

Reduction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> to <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> requires <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math> moles of electrons. Hence, charge required <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Faraday</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q65. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>For the reaction taking place in the cell:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mfenced open="||" close=""><mrow><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mfenced open="|" close=""><mrow><mi>Ag</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5332</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mo>&#8854;</mo></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. (in nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **51**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><menclose notation="bottom"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>Anode</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#10230;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>Cathode</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr></mtable></menclose><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mover><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Relation between Gibbs Free Energy and EMF of a Cell is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi>nFE</mi><mi>cell</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5332</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>51453</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>51</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4538</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cr</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>34</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>76</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>So Ans. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cu</mi></math></p>

### Q69. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The correct order of reduction potentials of the following pairs is</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math></p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Na</mi></math></p><p>E. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Li</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Li</mi></math></p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right left right left right left right left right left right left" columnspacing="0em 2em 0em 2em 0em 2em 0em 2em 0em 2em 0em" rowspacing="3pt"><mtr><mtd><mtext>&#160;</mtext><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8739;</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.36</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msup><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8739;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.54</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Li</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>|</mo><mi>Li</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.05</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msup><mrow><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#8739;</mo><mi>Ag</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.80</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>|</mo><mi>Na</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.71</mo></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>So, we can clearly see the order of reduction potential.</p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>In a cell, the following reactions take place</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mrow><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>The standard electrode potential for the spontaneous reaction in the cell is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>at</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is - (Nearest Integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **23**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The electrode which has more reduction potential will get reduced in spontaneous reaction in the cell. So, spontaneous reaction will be </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>Iron will act as cathode and Iodine will act as anode.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mi>RP</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mi>RP</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

In the industrial production of which of the following, molecular hydrogen is obtained as a bye product.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi></math> metal
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NaHCO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> is produced in Nelson cell using Brine solution.</p><p>Reaction taking place will be</p><p>Cathode: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>Anode: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>In solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>NaOH</mi></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> gas is released at cathode during preparation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following cell reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cd</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Hg</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>&#8652;</mo><msub><mi>CdSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Hg</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>.</mo></math>
The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>315</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math> If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>825</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> the standard entropy change <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;S</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is _________ . (Nearest integer)
[Given : Faraday constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>96487</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

For given cell reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>nFE</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mprescripts></mprescripts><mi>cell</mi><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>T&#916;S</mi></math>
Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>825</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mole</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>315</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96487</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;S</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>nFE</mi><msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#916;H</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;S</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96487</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>315</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>825</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mn>298</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>832</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>682</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>825</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mn>298</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7482</mn><mn>298</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;S</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></math>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2021 (22 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Assume a cell with the following reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>250</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Ag</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>cell</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>cell</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub></math> for the above reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><menclose notation="bottom"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></menclose></math>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>(Nearest integer)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo></math> Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3979</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>]</mo></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>059</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><msup><mfenced><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>81</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

</details>

### Q79. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Potassium chlorate is prepared by electrolysis of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi></math> in basic solution as shown by following equation.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p>A current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>xA</mi></math> has to be passed for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></math> to produce <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of potassium chlorate. the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is __________. (Nearest integer)</p><p>(Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KClO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>122</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>)</p>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>molKClO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is obtained.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> By the passage of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>molKClO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p><math
