### Q80. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>For the reaction
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
the magnitude of the standard molar free energy change, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo></math>______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>kJ</mi></math> (Round off to the Nearest Integer).</p><p><img height="81" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/25322c0c-f583-44c3-898c-7021b2474158-image.png" width="408" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **45**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Standard electrode potential is a measurement of the potential for equilibrium. There is a potential difference between the electrode and the electrolyte called the potential of the electrode. When unity is the concentrations of all the species involved in a semi-cell, the electrode potential is known as the standard electrode potential.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0112f096-adc8-41ca-afdb-607a7d478352-image.png" style="width: 262px; height: 108px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>036</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>772</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>cell&#160;</mtext><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>233</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>233</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p>Statement I : The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></math> value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>74</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>Statement II : Ce is more stable in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> state than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> state.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both statement I and statement II are correct
- **(B)** Statement I is incorrect but statement II is correct
- **(C)** Both statement I and statement II are incorrect
- **(D)** Statement I is correct but statement II is incorrect

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is correct but statement II is incorrect**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></math> value for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>74</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> because the most stable oxidation state of lanthanide series elements is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math>.</p><p>It means <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is more stable than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Consider the following reaction</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>The quantity of electricity required in Faraday to reduce five moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> is <u>             </u></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For this balanced reaction, the change in oxidation number of the metal ion is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p>Therefore one mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi></math> required <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> Faraday</p><p>Hence, for five mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi></math> required <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn></math> Faraday.</p>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Copper reduces <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> into <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> depending upon the concentration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> in solution. (Assuming fixed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi>NO</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub></math>), the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>HNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> concentration at which the thermodynamic tendency for reduction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> into <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> by copper is same is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mi>x</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>. - **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For concentration cell <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><munder><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></munder></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Anode : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>Cathode : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Overall : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></mrow><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>nF</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub></math></p><p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>ve</mi></math> then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub></math> is positive.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><munder><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></munder></msubsup><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.059</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.059</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Potassium chlorate is prepared by the electrolysis of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi></math> in basic solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msubsup><mi>ClO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> . If only <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of the current is utilized in the reaction, the time (rounded to the nearest hour) required to produce<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KClO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> using a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo></math> (Given :<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>500</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mol</mi></math>; molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KClO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>122</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **11**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Number of faradays = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mn>96500</mn></mfrac></math></p><p> =<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn></mrow><mn>96500</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p>Mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KClO</mi><mn>3</mn></math> produce = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Gm</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>{</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>}</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>122</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math>
So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>98</mn></math> hours</p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2020 (05 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>An oxidation-reduction reaction in which 3 electrons are transferred has a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>&#916;G</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>17</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>37</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math> (in V) is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **-6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>nF</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>17</mn><mo>.37</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.06</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of a waste solution obtained from the workshop of a goldsmith contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>AuCl</mi></math>. The solution was electrolyzed at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> by passing a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn></math> minutes. The metal/metals electrodeposited will be : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><mrow><msup><mtext>Ag</mtext><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mtext>Ag</mtext></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mtext>V</mtext><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><mrow><msup><mtext>Au</mtext><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mtext>Au</mtext></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>69</mn><mtext>V</mtext></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** only gold
- **(B)** silver and gold in proportion to their atomic weights
- **(C)** only silver
- **(D)** silver and gold in equal mass proportion

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) only gold**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#160;Charge&#160;</mtext><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>&#160;it&#160;</mtext><mn>96500</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn></mrow><mn>96500</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>900</mn><mn>96500</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>965</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0093</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p><p>No. of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Au</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>025</mn></math> &amp; No. of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>025</mn></math></p><p>Species with higher value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>SRP</mi></math> will get deposited first at cathode.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Au</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aqs</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#10230;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Au</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>025</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0093</mn></math> mole </p><p>so only <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Au</mi></math> will get deposited.</p>

</details>

---

### Q90. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8b399f50-be59-4ec7-b16f-e656ecc71134-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 420px;" />
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi>Cu</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.34</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi>Zn</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.76</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
Identify the incorrect statement from the options below for the above cell:

**Options:**

- **(A)** If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>ext</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo></msup></math> flow from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math>
- **(B)** If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>ext</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Zn</mi></math> dissolves at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math> electrode and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> deposits at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> electrode
- **(C)** If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>ext</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Zn</mi></math> dissolves at anode and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> deposits at cathode
- **(D)** If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>ext</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo></math> no flow of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo></msup></math> or current occurs

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>ext</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Zn</mi></math> dissolves at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math> electrode and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> deposits at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> electrode**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced open="|" close=""><mi>Cu</mi></mfenced></mrow><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>&#8722;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced open="|" close=""><mi>Zn</mi></mfenced></mrow><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p>So If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.1</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> no electron will flow</p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> cell act as electrolytic cell and electron will flow from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math>.</p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> cell act as electrochemical so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi></math> dissolve and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> deposit.</p>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>An acidic solution of dichromate is electrolyzed for 8 minutes using 2 A current. As per the following equation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>14</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
The amount of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>. obtained was <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>104</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>. The efficiency of the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>%</mo><mo>)</mo></math> is (Take : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>960000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>, At. mass of chromium <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>52</mn></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **60**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>it</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>960</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>960</mn><mn>96000</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>14</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msup><mi ### Q93. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 2)

### Q94. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<math><mn>108&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of silver (molar mass <math><mn>108&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) is deposited at cathode from <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> solution by a certain quantity of electricity. The volume (in <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> ) of oxygen gas produced at <math><mn>273&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and <math><mn>1</mn></math> bar pressure from water by the same quantity of electricity is ________

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5.66**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>p</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>108</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>108</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1&#160;</mn><mi></mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> charge is required to deposit <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mole</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>O</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#10230;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
<math><mn>2</mn><mi></mi><mi>F</mi></math> charge is required to deposit <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi></mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi></math>
<math><mn>1</mn><mi>F</mi></math> charge will deposit <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi></math> of <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>O</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>  gas.
<math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>n</mn><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mi></mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0821</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>273</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>22.4</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><msub><mi>O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5.6&#160;</mn><mi>L</mi></math></p><p>Faraday&#39;s second law of electrolysis states that, when the same quantity of electricity is passed through several electrolytes, the mass of the substances deposited are proportional to their respective equivalent weight.</p>

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For an electrochemical cell <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sn</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi>Sn</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>aq</mi><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>aq</mi><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi>Pb</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math> the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><msup><mi>Sn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></mfrac></math> when this cell attains equilibrium is _______
(Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mfenced open="" close="|" separators="|"><msup><mi>Sn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mi>Sn</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.14</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mfenced open="" close="|" separators="|"><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><mi>Pb</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.13</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.303</mo><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.06</mo></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2.15**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cell reaction is:

<math> <mrow> <mtext>Sn</mtext><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>s</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><msup> <mrow> <mtext>Pb</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>aq</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup> <mrow> <mtext>Sn</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>aq</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mtext>Pb</mtext><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>s</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow> </math>

Apply Nernst equation:

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>E</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>cell&#160;</mtext></mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo><msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>cell&#160;</mtext></mrow> <mn>0</mn> </msubsup> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>0.06</mn></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mi>log</mi><mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Sn</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Pb</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </mfrac> <mn>.....</mn><mtext>&#160;</mtext><mfenced> <mn>1</mn> </mfenced></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>cell&#160;</mtext></mrow> <mn>0</mn> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0.13</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.14</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.01</mn><mtext>V</mtext></mrow> </math>

At equilibrium : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><mtext>cell&#160;</mtext><mrow/></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> Substituting in (1)

<math> <mrow> <mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.01</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>0.06</mn></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mi>log</mi><mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Sn</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Pb</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo><mtext>&#8195;</mtext><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mi>log</mi><mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Sn</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Pb</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo><mtext>&#8195;</mtext><mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Sn</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>Pb</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>2.15</mn></mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

What would be the electrode potential for the given half-cell reaction at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>?</mo><mi></mi><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>.</mo></math>
<math><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mrow><mtext>H</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8853;</mo></mrow></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8.314</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>298</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">dard&#160;</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0.93**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>On applyinf Nernst equation-</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0591</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mi>logQ</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.23</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.0591</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>]</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></msup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.0591</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>p</mi><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.0591</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.2955</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.9345</mn><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.93</mn><mi>V</mi></math></p><p>The Nernst equation defines the relationship between cell potential to standard potential and to the activities of the electrically active (electroactive) species. It relates the effective concentrations (activities) of the components of a cell reaction to the standard cell potential.</p>

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given that the standard potentials <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cu</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cu</mi></math> are <math><mn>0.34</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and <math><mn>0.522</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> respectively, the <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0.182</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>+</mo><mn>0.158</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.182</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>0.158</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>+</mo><mn>0.158</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/01bfe3e2-7ea0-4cb5-a16d-355302432e22.png"> 
<math><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0.34</mn><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0.522</mn><mi></mi></math>  
<math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0.68</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0.522</mn></math>  
<math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0.158</mn></math>

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A solution of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> is electrolyzed between platinum electrode <math><mn>0.1</mn></math> Faraday electricity. How many mole of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> will be deposited at the cathode?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0.10</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>0.15</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>0.05</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>0.20</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>0.05</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math>
<math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> charge gives = 1 mole of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>
<math><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> charge gives <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.05</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard Gibbs energy for the given cell reaction in <math><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> at <math><mn>298</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> is:
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math> ,
<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>298</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
<math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#39;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>192</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>192</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>384</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>384</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mo>-</mo><mn>384</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the reaction,
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10230;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, two electrons are exchanged
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>384000</mn></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>384</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Calculate the standard cell potential (in V) of the cell in which the following reaction takes place:</p><p><math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Given that
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mover><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p><p><math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> of cathode <math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> of anode
<math><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup></math></p><p>Since <math><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></math> cell is an intensive property, the <math><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></math> cell values are not additive by nature. But the standard Gibbs free energy change is an extensive property and hence can be added.</p><p>(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>;</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>z</mi></math></p><p>(ii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>Subtracting (ii) from (i)
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math></p><p><math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>The standard electrode potential <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>o</mo></msup></math> and its temperature coefficient <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mi>dE</mi><mi>dT</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math> for a cell are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8201;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><msup><mo>&#215;10</mo><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300&#8201;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, respectively. The reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></math>. The standard reaction enthalpy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#8201;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> in <math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is </p><p><math><mo>[</mo></math>Use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=96,500&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8201;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>]</mo></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>412.8</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>206.4</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>384.0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>192.0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mo>-</mo><mn>412.8</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mtext>G</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>nFE</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>386</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mtext>S</mtext><mo>=</mo><mi>nF</mi><mfrac><mi>dE</mi><mi>dT</mi></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>degree</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>celcious</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>.5&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298&#8201;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T&#916;</mi><mtext>S</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>298</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>.5</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>.757</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>
At constant temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>248</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and pressure,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>T&#916;</mi><mtext>S</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;G</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>T&#916;</mi><mtext>S</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>386</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>28.757</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>-414.75</mn><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>-412.8</mn></math>

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given the equilibrium constant:

$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}$ of the reaction:

$\mathrm{Cu}(\mathrm{s})+2 \mathrm{Ag}^{+}(\mathrm{aq}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})+2 \mathrm{Ag}(\mathrm{s})$ is

$10 \times 10^{15}$ calculate the $E_{\text {cell }}^{0}$ of this reaction at $298 \mathrm{~K}$ $\left[2.303 \frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{F}}\right.$ at $\left.298 \mathrm{~K}=0.059 \mathrm{~V}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.04736 \mathrm{mV}$
- **(B)** $0.4736 \mathrm{mV}$
- **(C)** $0.4736 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.04736 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.4736 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$E_{\text {cell }}^{0}=\frac{2.303 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{nF}} \log \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}$ or $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{0}=\frac{0.059 \mathrm{~V}}{\mathrm{n}} \log \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{C}}$

$$

=\frac{0.059 \mathrm{~V}}{2} \log 10^{16}=0.4736 \mathrm{~V}

$$

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

For the cell $\mathrm{Zn}(\mathrm{s})\left|\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq}) \| \mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{x}+}(\mathrm{aq})\right| \mathrm{M}(\mathrm{s}),$ different half

cells and their standard electrode potentials are given below:

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8_ywllHaThmbgd-amuF-6N-zUeHl1_cT31EEiZ4jxW4.original.fullsize.png">


If $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Zn}^{2+} / \mathrm{Zn}}^{\circ}=-0.76 \mathrm{~V},$ which cathode will give a maximum value of $E_{\text {cell }}^{0}$ per electron transferred?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+} / \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Au}^{3+} / \mathrm{Au}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} / \mathrm{Fe}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Zn}+2 \mathrm{Ag}^{+} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Ag}+\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\left(n=2 e^{-}\right)$

$=0.80-(-0.76)=1.56 \mathrm{~V}$ for $2 e$

$\therefore \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ for $1 e^{-}=\frac{1.56}{2}=0.78 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

In the cell, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>1</mn><mi>bar</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>HCl</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>AgCl</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi>Ag</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mfenced close="" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Pt</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>the cell potential is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.92</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> when a <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></math> molar <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> solution is used. The standard electrode potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi><mo>|</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>|</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> electrode is:

(Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.303</mo><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.06</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.76</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.20</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.40</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.94</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.20</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.0</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>AgCl</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Ag</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>xV</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.06</mo></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.92</mo><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mi>log</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.92</mo><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.92</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.72</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q105. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following reduction processes:

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Zn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.76</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ca</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.87</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.36</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.25</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

The reducing power of the metals increases in the order:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ni</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Lower the standard reduction potential more will be the reducing power i.e gets self oxidised.

Therefore the correct order is:

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi></math>

</details>
