### Q106. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

If the standard electrode potential for a cell is <math><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>V</mi></math> at <math><mn>300</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>,</mo></math> the equilibrium constant <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> for the reaction.

<math><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8652;</mo><mi>Z</mi><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>u</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi></math>

at <math><mn>300</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>K</mi></math> is approximately:

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>J</mi><msup><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96000</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>160</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>80</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>160</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>320</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>160</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>Q</mi></math>
At equilibrium <math><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math>
<math><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>K</mi></math>
<math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>F</mi><msup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
So <math><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>F</mi><msup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>k</mi></math>
<math><mi>&#8242;</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>&#8242;</mi></math> factor for the above cell <math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
So <math><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>k</mi></math>
<math><mi>l</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>160</mn></math>
<math><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>160</mn></mrow></msup></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The anodic half-cell of lead-acid battery is recharged using electricity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.05</mo></math> Faraday. The amount of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PbSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> electrolyzed in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> during the process is: (Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PbSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>303</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>22.8</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>15.2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>11.4</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>7.6</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>7.6</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Charge of one mole of  <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> faraday

 <math><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>S</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>→</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> 

If  <math><mn>2</mn><mi>F</mi></math>  charge is passed then amount of  <math><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi></math> deposited  <math><mo>=</mo><mn>303</mn><mi> </mi><mi>g</mi></math> 

So if  <math><mn>0.05</mn><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi></math>  faraday current passed then  <math><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>  deposited  <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>303</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></math> 

 <math><mo>=</mo><mn>7.575</mn></math> 

 <math><mo>≈</mo><mn>7.6</mn><mi> </mi><mi>g</mi></math>

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When an electric current is passed through acidified water, 112 mL of hydrogen gas at N.T.P was collected at the cathode in 965 seconds. The current passed, in ampere, is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2.0
- **(B)** 0.1
- **(C)** 0.5
- **(D)** 1.0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 1.0**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Cathode
<math><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mo>&#10230;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><mi>O</mi><msup><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> <math><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
Mole <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>112</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>22400</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>965</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>p</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q109. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

How long (approximate) should water be electrolysed by passing through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>amperes</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>current so that the oxygen released can completely burn <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>66</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of diborane? (Atomic weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>hours</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>hours</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>hours</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>hours</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>hours</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
According to balanced equation
27.66 g <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></math> i.e. 1 mole <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></math> requires 3 mole of <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> . Now this oxygen is produced by electrolysis of water as follows
<math><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mover accent="false"><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mover><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
As 1 mole <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> is produced by 4 F charge
So 3 mole <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> will be produced by 12 F charge
So now on applying
Q <math><mo>=</mo></math> It
<math><mtext>12</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>965</mtext><mn>00</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96500</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3600</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> hours
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3.2</mn></math> hours

</details>

---

### Q110. JEE Main 2017 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

To find the standard potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> electrode, the following cell is constituted: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.001</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>mol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.01</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>mol</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>/</mo><mi>Ag</mi></math>

The emf of the cell is found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>421</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The standard potential of half-reaction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> will be:

(Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfrac bevelled="true"><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mi>Ag</mi></mfrac><mo>&#8861;</mo></msubsup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>38</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>66</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>volt</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0591</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>logQ</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced><msup><mfenced><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.001</mo></mrow><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.01</mo><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0591</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>log</mi><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0591</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.421</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.0591</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>421</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi>cell</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mrow><msup><mi>Ag</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Ag</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2017 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following ions does not liberate hydrogen gas on reaction with dilute acids?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ti</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> does not displace <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> from dilute acid because they are less reactive <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>SRP</mi><mo>&#8201;</mo><mi>value</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>. </p><p>Given below are SRP values,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>|</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8277</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>|</mo><mi>Mn</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>185</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q112. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

What is the standard reduction potential <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></mfenced></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>?</mo></math>

Given that:

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Fe</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.47</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.77</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.057</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.30</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.30</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.057</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.057</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math>;

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math> factor<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>&#916;G</mtext><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math>; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math> factor<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Fe</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn></math> 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math> factor<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">G</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;G</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#916;G</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#916;G</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><msubsup><mi>FE</mi><msup><mrow></mrow><mn>1</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.77</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.47</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>-E</mtext><msup><mrow></mrow><mn>1</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.47</mo></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.77</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.057</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><msup><mrow></mrow><mn>1</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.057</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q113. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following standard electrode potentials (<math><msup><mrow><mtext>E</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>o</mtext></mrow></msup></math> in volts) in aqueous solution:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><munder><mrow><mtext>E</mtext><mtext>l</mtext><mtext>e</mtext><mtext>m</mtext><mtext>e</mtext><mtext>n</mtext><mtext>t</mtext></mrow><mrow></mrow></munder></mtd><mtd><munder><mrow><msup><mtext>M</mtext><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mtext>M</mtext></mrow><mrow></mrow></munder></mtd><mtd><munder><mrow><msup><mtext>M</mtext><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>/</mo><mtext>M</mtext></mrow><mrow></mrow></munder></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mtext>A</mtext><mtext>l</mtext></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1.66</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo><mn>0.55</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mtext>T</mtext><mtext>l</mtext></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo><mn>1.26</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>0.34</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>
Based on these data, which of the following statements is correct?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mtext>T</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is more stable than <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  is more stable than  <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mtext>T</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is more stable than <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mtext>T</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is more stable than <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mtext>T</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is more stable than <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">G</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">o</mtext></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="normal">n</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">F</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">E</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">o</mtext></mrow></msup></math>

if <math><msup><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">E</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">o</mtext></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">p</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">o</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">s</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">i</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">t</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">i</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">v</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">e</mtext><mo>;</mo><mtext mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mtext></math>then <math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">G</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext mathvariant="normal">o</mtext></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="normal">n</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">e</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">g</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">a</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">t</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">i</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">v</mtext><mtext mathvariant="normal">e</mtext></math>,

For those, conversions whose <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </math> values are more positive are more favourable.
As we can see from then electrode potential data, that reduction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is spontaneous, but reduction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Tl</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is not. So, we can conclude from this that <math><mtext>T</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is more stable than <math><mtext>A</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>l</mtext></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q114. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.36</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cr</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.74</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msub><mi>Cr</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.33</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msubsup><mi>MnO</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.51</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>.</mo></math>
Among the following, the strongest reducing agent is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Strongest reducing agent has the highest tendency to get oxidised. Higher the oxidation potential, higher is the tendency to get oxidised. Reduction potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is least (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>Cr</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.74</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>) which indicates that oxidation potential of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math> is highest (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.74</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>). Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math> is strongest reducing agent.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q115. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Oxidation of succinate ion produces ethylene and carbon dioxide gases. On passing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math> Faraday electricity through an aqueous solution of potassium succinate, what is the total volume of gases (at both cathode and anode) at STP (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>273</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>)?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/135631_1.1.jpg" style="width: 477px; height: 108px;" />

Ethene, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are gases.</p><p>So total moles of gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>

By <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> Farady<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> mole of gas produced

So, by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math> Farady <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></math> mole gas produced

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.0821</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>273</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8.96</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

What will happen when a block of copper metal is dropped into a beaker containing a solution of <math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>M</mtext></mrow> </math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** The copper metal will dissolve with evolution of oxygen gas.
- **(B)** The copper metal will dissolve with evolution of hydrogen gas.
- **(C)** No reaction will occur.
- **(D)** The copper metal will dissolve and zinc metal will be deposited.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) No reaction will occur.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Zn is above Cu in electrochemical series. So, Zn will reduce Cu<sup>2+</sup> to Cu.
Cu + Zn<sup>2+</sup> &rarr; No reaction
Displacement reaction is feasible in opposite direction only.
Cu<sup>2+</sup> + Zn  &rarr; Zn<sup>2+</sup> + Cu <math><mo>&#8595;</mo></math>

</details>

---

### Q117. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, the standard reduction potentials are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> for <math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> for <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>07</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> for <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> for <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>|</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>. At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>pH</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>, permanganate is expected to oxidize: <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.059</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>  only
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.059</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">log</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mrow></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.51</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.059</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">log</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mrow></math>
(Assuming <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> )
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.51</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.059</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1.51</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0.28</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mfrac></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math>
i.e. it will oxidise <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>, only.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A variable, the opposite external potential <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>ext</mi></msub><mo>)</mo></math> is applied to the cell <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8741;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>|</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></math>, of potential <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. When <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, respectively electrons flow from

**Options:**

- **(A)** anode to cathode in both the cases.
- **(B)** anode to cathode and cathode to anode.
- **(C)** cathode to anode and anode to cathode.
- **(D)** cathode to anode in both cases.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) anode to cathode and cathode to anode.**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a Daniel cell,

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.34</mn><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.76</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1.10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>.

When <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1.10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, electrons flow from anode to cathode in the external circuit.

When <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1.10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, electrons flow from cathode to anode in the external circuit (reverse reaction).

</details>

### Q119. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Two Faraday of electricity is passed through a solution of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math> . The mass of copper deposited at the cathode is: (Atomic mass of Cu = 63.5 amu)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>127</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>63.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>63.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cu</mi></math></p><p>2 Faraday charge deposits 1 mol of Cu, or</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Equivalents</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>Cu</mo></math>

Mass of Cu deposited<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>63</mn><mo>.5</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>.5</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given
$$
\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq}) ; \mathrm{E}^0=+0.77 \mathrm{~V}
$$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\mathrm{Al}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq})+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Al}(\mathrm{s}) ; \mathrm{E}^0=-1.66 \mathrm{~V} \\
&\mathrm{Br}_2(\mathrm{aq})+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Br}^{-} ; \mathrm{E}^0=+1.09 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$
Considering the electrode potentials, which of the following represents the correct order of reducing power?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} < \mathrm{A} l < \mathrm{Br}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Br}^{-} < \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} < \mathrm{Al}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Al} < \mathrm{Br}^{-} < \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Al} < \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} < \mathrm{Br}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{Al} < \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} < \mathrm{Br}^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reducing character decreases down the series. Hence the correct order is
$$
\mathrm{Al} < \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} < \mathrm{Br}^{-}
$$

</details>

### Q121. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The standard electrode potentials <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>M</mtext> <mo>+</mo> </msup> <mo>/</mo> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mtext>o</mtext> </msubsup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math> of four metals <math> <mrow> <mtext>A,</mtext><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>B,</mtext><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>C</mtext></mrow> </math> and <math> <mtext>D</mtext> </math> are <math> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mtext>1</mtext><mtext>.2</mtext><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>V,</mtext></mrow> </math><math> <mrow> <mtext>0</mtext><mtext>.6</mtext><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></mrow> </math>, <math> <mrow> <mtext>0</mtext><mtext>.85</mtext><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mtext>0</mtext><mtext>.76</mtext><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></mrow> </math>, respectively. The sequence of deposition of metals on applying potential is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In complete cation electrolysis at cathode, the species having higher reduction potential is preferably reduced at cathode
<math> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>C</mtext> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msubsup> <mo>&#62;</mo> <msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>B</mtext> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msubsup> <mo>&#62;</mo> <msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>D</mtext> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msubsup> <mo>&#62;</mo> <msubsup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mtext>A</mtext> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>
Hence,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
will be the preference of deposition of these metals on competing.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>A current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> flows for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></math> through an electrolytic cell containing a molten salt of metal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math>. This results in the decomposition of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of metal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> at the cathode. The oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> in the molten salt is:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Quantity of Electricity <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mn>6</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>9</mn> <mn>6</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
                                   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.746</mo><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.75</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>.
If oxidation state is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> deposition of a metal:
Electricity<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>nF</mi></math> (Faradays)
For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0.250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>nF</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0.250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>nF</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0.75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>F</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q123. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are the half - cell reactions :

<math> <mrow> <mtable> <mtr> <mtd> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> <mo>→</mo> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mo> ; </mo> </mtd> <mtd> <msup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mo>∘</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>1.18 V</mtext> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>2</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> <mo>→</mo> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo> ; </mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mo>∘</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>1.51 V</mtext> </mrow> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </mrow> </math>

The <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mo>∘</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math> for <math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> <mo>→</mo> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>2.69 V</mtext> </mrow> </math> ; the reaction will not occur
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>2.69 V</mtext> </mrow> </math> ; the reaction will occur
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>0.33 V</mtext> </mrow> </math> ; the reaction will not occur
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>0.33 V</mtext> </mrow> </math> ; the reaction will occur

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>2.69 V</mtext> </mrow> </math> ; the reaction will not occur**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mrow><mo>&#916;</mo> <msup> <mtext>G</mtext> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>nF</mtext><msup> <mtext>E</mtext> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mtext>Mn</mtext> </mrow> </math>   ........... (1)

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>G</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>1.18</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow><mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>2.36 F</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mo>-</mo> </msup> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>   ....... (2)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>G</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>F</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mtext>+1.51</mtext></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mtext>3.02 F</mtext></math>

(1) - (2)

<math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mtext>Mn</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>G</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>2.36F</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","><mrow>&#160;<mtext>3.02F</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>5.38 F</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>G</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>E</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>E</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>5.38F</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>F</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>2.69 V</mtext> </mrow> </math>

We know that when E<sub>cell</sub> &lt; 0, cell is non-spontaneous.

</details>

### Q124. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The metal that cannot be obtained by the electrolysis of an aqueous solution of its salt is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the reduction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> at cathode,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>OH</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>red</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ca</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi>red</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.87</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

i.e., water will be reduced at the cathode, in preference to the liberation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi></math>.

However, in the case of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ag</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cu</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cr</mi></math>, their standard reduction potentials are higher than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> and will be liberated/ deposited at the cathode and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> will not be reduced.

</details>

### Q125. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The Gibbs energy for the decomposition of $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$ at $500 \mathrm{~C}$ is as follows :
$\frac{2}{3} \mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 \rightarrow \frac{4}{3} \mathrm{Al}+\mathrm{O}_2, \Delta_r G=+940 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
The potential difference needed for the electrolytic reduction of aluminium oxide at $500^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ should be at least :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.5 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $3.0 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $5.0 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $2.5 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3.0 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In the reaction
$\frac{2}{3} \mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 \longrightarrow \frac{4}{3} \mathrm{Al}+\mathrm{O}_2$
For the oxidation half-reaction
$\mathrm{Al}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}$
no. of electron transfered $(n)=3$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-n \mathrm{FE}^{\circ} \\
& 940=3 \times 96500 \times \mathrm{E}^{\circ}
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{E}^{\circ} & =\frac{940 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~J}}{3 \times 96500} \\
& =3.24 \approx 3 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q126. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given :
$$
\begin{gathered}
\mathrm{E}_{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Cl}_2 / \mathrm{Cl}^{-}}^{\mathrm{o}}=1.36 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}^{3+} / \mathrm{Cr}}^{\mathrm{o}}=-0.74 \mathrm{~V} \\
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-} / \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}}^{\mathrm{o}}=1.33 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-} / \mathrm{Mn}^{2+}}^{\mathrm{o}}=1.51 \mathrm{~V}
\end{gathered}
$$
The correct order of reducing power of the species $\left(\mathrm{Cr}, \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}, \mathrm{Mn}^{2+}\right.$ and $\left.\mathrm{Cl}^{-}\right)$will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{Cr}^{3+} < \mathrm{Cr}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{3+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{Cr}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{Mn}^{2+} < \mathrm{Cr}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{Cr} < \mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{Cr}^{3+} < \mathrm{Cr}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Lower the value of reduction potential higher will be reducing power hence the correct order will be
$$
\mathrm{Mn}^{2+} < \mathrm{Cl}^{-} < \mathrm{Cr}^{3+} < \mathrm{Cr}
$$

</details>

### Q127. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Electrode potentials $\left(\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\right)$ are given below :
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Cu}^{+} / \mathrm{Cu}=+0.52 \mathrm{~V} ,\\
& \mathrm{Fe}^{3+} / \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}=+0.77 \mathrm{~V}, \\
& \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~s}) / \mathrm{I}^{-}=+0.54 \mathrm{~V}, \\
& \mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}=+0.88 \mathrm{~V}.
\end{aligned}$
Based on the above potentials, strongest oxidizing agent will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Cu}^{+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Ag}^{+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{I}_2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{Ag}^{+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Higher the value of reduction potential stronger will be the oxidising hence based on the given values $\mathrm{Ag}^{+}$will be strongest oxidizing agent.

</details>

### Q128. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Four successive members of the first row of transition elements are listed below with atomic numbers. Which one of them is expected to have the highest <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mtext>E</mtext><mrow><msup><mtext>M</mtext><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msup><mtext>M</mtext><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup></math> value?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Fe (Z = 26)
- **(B)** Co (Z = 27)
- **(C)** Cr (Z = 24)
- **(D)** Mn (Z = 25)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Co (Z = 27)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Species having the highest reduction potential i.e., tendency to undergo reduction by gain of electron. SRP value increases from left to right in the period for the d-block elements.

### Q129. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard potentials of $\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}, \mathrm{Hg}_2{ }^{2+} / 2 \mathrm{Hg}$, $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} / \mathrm{Cu}$ and $\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} / \mathrm{Mg}$ electrodes are $0.80,0.79$, $0.34$ and $-2.37 \mathrm{~V}$, respectively. An aqueous solution which contains one mole per litre of the salts of each of the four metals is electrolyzed. With increasing voltage, the correct sequence of deposition of the metals at the cathode is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Ag}, \mathrm{Hg}, \mathrm{Cu}, \mathrm{Mg}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cu}, \mathrm{Hg}$, Ag only
- **(C)** Ag, Hg, Cu only
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Mg}, \mathrm{Cu}, \mathrm{Hg}, \mathrm{Ag}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Ag, Hg, Cu only**

**Detailed Solution:**

More the reduction potential, more is the deposition of metals at cathode. Cation having $\mathrm{E}^0$ value less than $-0.83 \mathrm{~V}$ (reduction potential of $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$ ) will not deposit from aqueous solution. Hence correct order of A deposition of the metal at the cathode is

</details>

### Q130. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A battery is constructed of $\mathrm{Cr}$ and $\mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7$. The unbalanced chemical equation when such a battery discharges is following:
$$
\mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7+\mathrm{Cr}+\mathrm{H}^{+} \rightarrow \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}+\mathrm{Na}^{+}
$$
If one Faraday of electricity is passed through the battery during the charging, the number of moles of $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$ removed from the solution is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{4}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{3}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reduction half reaction :
$$
\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}+6 e^{-}+14 \mathrm{H}^{+} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}+7 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}
$$
Oxidation half reaction :
$$
\mathrm{Cr} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}+3 e^{-}
$$
Overall reaction :
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}+\mathrm{Cr}+14 \mathrm{H}^{+}+3 e^{-} \\
& 3 \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}+7 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}
\end{aligned}
$$
$3 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity $=3$ moles of $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$
$1 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity $=\frac{3}{3}$ moles of $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$

</details>

### Q131. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The standard reduction potentials for $\mathrm{Zn}^{2+} / \mathrm{Zn}, \mathrm{Ni}^{2+} / \mathrm{Ni}$, and $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} / \mathrm{Fe}$ are $-0.76,-0.23$ and $-0.44 \mathrm{~V}$ respectively. The reaction $\mathrm{X}+\mathrm{Y}^{2+} \rightarrow \mathrm{X}^{2+}+\mathrm{Y}$ will be spontaneous when:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $X=N i, Y=F e$
- **(B)** $X=N i, Y=Z n$
- **(C)** $X=F e, Y=Z n$
- **(D)** $X=Z n, Y=N i$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $X=Z n, Y=N i$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Zn}+\mathrm{Fe}^{+2} \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}^{+2}+\mathrm{Fe} \\
& \mathrm{Fe}+\mathrm{Ni}^{+2} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+\mathrm{Ni} \\
& \mathrm{Zn}+\mathrm{Ni}^{2+} \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}^{+2}+\mathrm{Ni}
\end{aligned}$
All these are spontaneous

</details>

### Q133. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The Gibbs energy for the decomposition of $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$ at $500^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ is as follows :
$$
\frac{2}{3} \mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 \rightarrow \frac{4}{3} \mathrm{Al}+\mathrm{O}_2, \Delta_{\mathrm{r}} \mathrm{G}=+966 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
$$
The potential difference needed for electrolytic reduction of $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$ at $500^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ is at least

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.5 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $3.0 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $2.5 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $5.0 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2.5 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta G=-n F E \quad \Rightarrow E=\frac{-\Delta G}{n F}$
$E=-\frac{966 \times 10^3}{4 \times 96500}$
$=-2.5 \mathrm{~V}$
$\therefore$ The potential difference needed for the reduction $=2.5 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q134. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given : $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{3+} / \mathrm{Fe}}^{\circ}=-0.036 \mathrm{~V}, \quad \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} / \mathrm{Fe}}^{\circ}=-0.439 \mathrm{~V}$. The value of standard electrode potential for the change, $\mathrm{Fe}_{(\text {aq })}^{3+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})$ will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-0.072 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.385 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $0.770 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.270$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.770 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \because \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe} ; \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.036 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \therefore \Delta \mathrm{G}_1^{\circ}=-\mathrm{nFE}^{\circ}=-3 \mathrm{~F}(-0.036) \\
& =+0.108 \mathrm{~F} \\
& \text { Also } \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe} ; \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.439 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \therefore \Delta \mathrm{G}_2^{\circ}=-\mathrm{nFE}^{\circ} \\
& =-2 \mathrm{~F}(-0.439) \\
& =0.878 \mathrm{~F} \\
& \text { To find } \mathrm{E}^{\circ} \text { for } \mathrm{Fe}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{3+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq}) \\
& \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-\mathrm{nFE}{ }^{\circ}=-1 \mathrm{FE} \circ \\
& \because \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=\mathrm{G}_1^{\circ}-\mathrm{G}_2^{\circ} \\
& \therefore \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=0.108 \mathrm{~F}-0.878 \mathrm{~F} \\
& \therefore-\mathrm{FE}^{\circ}=+0.108 \mathrm{~F}-0.878 \mathrm{~F} \\
& \therefore \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=0.878-0.108 \\
& =0.77 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q135. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

In a fuel cell methanol is used as fuel and oxygen gas is used as an oxidizer. The reaction is $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}(\ell)+\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell)$ At $298 \mathrm{~K}$ standard Gibb's energies of formation for $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}(\ell), \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell)$ and $\mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})$ are $-166.2,-237.2$ and $-394.4 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively. If standard enthalpy of combustion of methanol is $-726 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, efficiency of the fuel cell will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $80 \%$
- **(B)** $87 \%$
- **(C)** $90 \%$
- **(D)** $97 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $97 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}(\ell)+\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}=-726 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
$$
Also $\Delta \mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{f}}^0 \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{OH}(\ell)=-166.2 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{f}}^0 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell)=-237.2 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{f}}^0 \mathrm{CO}_2(\ell)=-394.4 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \because \Delta \mathrm{G}=\Sigma \Delta \mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{f}}^0 \text { products }-\Sigma \Delta \mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{f}}^0 \text { reactants. } \\
& =-394.4-2(237.2)+166.2 \\
& =-702.6 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}
$$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { now Efficiency of fuel cell }=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{G}}{\Delta \mathrm{H}} \times 100 \\
& =\frac{702.6}{726} \times 100 \\
& =97 \%
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>
