### Q136. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr}^3+/ \mathrm{Cr}}^{\circ}=-0.72 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{2+/ / \mathrm{Fe}}}^{\circ}=-0.42 \mathrm{~V}$. The potential for the cell $\mathrm{Cr}\left|\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}(0.1 \mathrm{M})\right|\left|\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(0.01 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Fe}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.26 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.399 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.339 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.26 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.26 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { As } \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cr} / \mathrm{cr}^{3+}}^0=-0.72 \mathrm{~V} \text { and } \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} / \mathrm{Fe}}^0=-0.42 \mathrm{~V} \\
& 2 \mathrm{Cr}+3 \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} \longrightarrow 3 \mathrm{Fe}+2 \mathrm{Cr}^{3+} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0-\frac{0.0591}{6} \log \frac{\left(\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}\right)^2}{\left(\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}\right)^3} \\
& =(-0.42+0.72)-\frac{0.0591}{6} \log \frac{(0.1)^2}{(0.01)^3}=0.30-\frac{0.0591}{6} \log \frac{(0.1)^2}{(0.01)^3} \\
& =0.30-\frac{0.0591}{6} \log \frac{10^{-2}}{10^{-6}}=0.30-\frac{0.0591}{6} \log 10^4 \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=0.2606 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q137. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The cell, $\mathrm{Zn}\left|\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M}) \| \mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=1.10 \mathrm{~V}\right)$, was allowed to be completely discharged at $\mathrm{298~K}$. The relative concentration of $\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}$ to $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\left[\frac{\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\right]}\right]$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** antilog $(24.08)$
- **(B)** $37.3$
- **(C)** $10^{37.3}$
- **(D)** $9.65 \times 10^4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $10^{37.3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\circ}-\frac{0.0591}{\mathrm{n}} \log \mathrm{Q} \\
& \text { Where } \mathrm{Q}=\frac{\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\right]}
\end{aligned}$
For complete discharge $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=0$
So $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.591}{2} \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\right]}$
$\Rightarrow\left|\frac{\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\right]}\right|=10^{37.3}$
Hence, (C) is correct.

</details>

### Q138. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given the data at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Ag}+\mathrm{I}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{AgI}+\mathrm{e}^{-} ; \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=0.152 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \mathrm{Ag} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ag}^{+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} ; \quad \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.800 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$
What is the value of $\log \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{sp}}$ for $\mathrm{AgI}$ ?
$$
\left(2.303 \frac{R T}{F}=0.059 \mathrm{~V}\right)
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-8.12$
- **(B)** $+8.612$
- **(C)** $-37.83$
- **(D)** $-16.13$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-16.13$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{array}{ll}
\mathrm{AgI}(\mathrm{s})+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Ag}(\mathrm{s})+\mathrm{I}^{-} ; & \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.152 \\
\mathrm{Ag}(\mathrm{s}) \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ag}^{+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} ; & \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.8 \\
\hline \mathrm{AgI}(\mathrm{s}) \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ag}^{+}+\mathrm{I}^{-} ; & \mathrm{E}^{\circ}=-0.952 \\
\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\circ}=\frac{0.059}{\mathrm{n}} \operatorname{logK} & \\
-0.952=\frac{0.059}{1} \log \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{sp}} \\
\log \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{sp}}=-\frac{0.952}{0.059}=-16.135
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q141. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard e.m.f of a cell, involving one electron change is found to be $0.591 \mathrm{~V}$ at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The equilibrium constant of the reaction is $\left(\mathrm{F}=96,500 \mathrm{C} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}: \mathrm{R}=8.314 \mathrm{JK}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.0 \times 10^1$
- **(B)** $1.0 \times 10^{30}$
- **(C)** $1.0 \times 10^{10}$
- **(D)** $1.0 \times 10^5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.0 \times 10^{10}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\therefore \log \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}=\frac{\mathrm{nE}^{\circ}}{0.0591}
=\frac{1 \times 0.591}{0.0591}$ $\Rightarrow \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{eq}}=10^{10}$

</details>

### Q142. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Consider the following $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ values
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{3+} / \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}}^{\circ}=0.77 \mathrm{~V} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Sn}^{2+} / \mathrm{Sn}}^{\circ}=-0.14 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$
Under standard conditions the potential for the reaction
$\mathrm{Sn}(\mathrm{s})+2 \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}(\mathrm{aq}) \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{Sn}^{2+}(\mathrm{aq})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.68 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.63 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $0.91 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $1.40 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.91 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{RHS}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{LHS}}^{\circ}$
$=(0.77)-(-0.14)$
$=0.91 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q143. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For the redox reaction $\mathrm{Zn}(\mathrm{s})+\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(0.1 \mathrm{M}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M})+\mathrm{Cu}(\mathrm{s})$ taking place in a cell, $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0$ is $1.10$ volt. $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$
for the cell will be $\left(2.303 \frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{F}}=0.0591\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.80$ volt
- **(B)** $1.07$ volt
- **(C)** $0.82$ volt
- **(D)** $2.14$ volt

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.07$ volt**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0+\frac{0.059}{\mathrm{n}} \log \left[\frac{\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}}{\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}}\right]$
$=1.10+\frac{0.059}{2} \log [0.1]=1.10-0.0295=1.07 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q144. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When during electrolysis of a solution of $\mathrm{AgNO}_3 9650$ coulombs of charge pass through the electroplating bath, the mass of silver deposited on the cathode will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $10.8 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(B)** $21.6 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(C)** $108 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(D)** $1.08 \mathrm{~g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $10.8 \mathrm{~g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

No. of moles of silver $=\frac{9650}{96500}=\frac{1}{10}$ moles
$\therefore$ Mass of silver deposited $=\frac{1}{10} \times 108=10.8 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q146. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For a cell reaction involving a two-electron change, the standard e.m.f. of the cell is found to be $0.295 \mathrm{~V}$ at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The equilibrium constant of the reaction at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $29.5 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(B)** 10
- **(C)** $1 \times 10^{10}$
- **(D)** $1 \times 10^{-10}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1 \times 10^{-10}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equilibrium constant is related to the standard emf of cell by the expression
$\log K=E_{\text {cell }}^0 \times \frac{n}{0.059}=0.295 \times \frac{2}{0.059}$
$\log \mathrm{K}=\frac{590}{59}=10$ or $\mathrm{K}=1 \times 10^{-10}$

</details>

### Q147. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

When the sample of copper with zinc impurity is to be purified by electrolysis, the appropriate electrodes are

**Options:**

- **(A)** cathode - pure zinc     anode - pure copper
- **(B)** cathode - impure sample     anode - pure copper
- **(C)** cathode - impure zinc     anode - impure sample
- **(D)** cathode - pure copper     anode - impure sample

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) cathode - pure copper     anode - impure sample**

**Detailed Solution:**

Pure metal always deposits at cathode

</details>

### Q149. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

If $\phi$ denotes reduction potential, then which is true ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $E_{\text {cell }}^0=\phi_{\text {right }}-\phi_{\text {left }}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\phi_{\text {left }}+\phi_{\text {right }}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\phi_{\text {left }}-\phi_{\text {right }}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=-\left(\phi_{\text {left }}+\phi_{\text {right }}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $E_{\text {cell }}^0=\phi_{\text {right }}-\phi_{\text {left }}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$E_{\text {cell }}=E_{\text {right (cathode) }}-E_{\text {left (anode) }}$

</details>

### Q150. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

EMF of a cell in terms of reduction potential of its left and right electrodes is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $E=E_{\text {left }}-E_{\text {right }}$
- **(B)** $E=E_{\text {left }}+E_{\text {right }}$
- **(C)** $E=E_{\text {right }}-E_{\text {left }}$
- **(D)** $E=-\left(E_{\text {right }}+E_{\text {left }}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $E=E_{\text {right }}-E_{\text {left }}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }} & =\text { Reduction potential of cathode (right) - reduction potential of anode (left) } \\ & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {right }}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {left }}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---
